#### [A] Procaine and Doxorubicin Coloaded Microspheres for Painless and Potent Transarterial Chemoembolization.

- PMID: 42994449
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: ACS applied materials & interfaces
- 作者: Tian C, Wang W, Cai D, Zhang H, Li X, Liu S, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 09
- DOI: 10.1021/acsami.6c15393
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42994449/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Transarterial chemoembolization (TACE) is the standard treatment for intermediate-stage hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC). However, its clinical efficacy is severely limited by postembolization syndrome (PES) and the physical vascular barrier induced by embolization. To address these limitations, we developed multifunctional polyvinyl alcohol embolization microspheres modified with polydopamine and coloaded with procaine (PCA) and doxorubicin (DOX), termed pDA-PVA/PCA-DOX. MD/MM-PBSA simulations and physicochemical characterization confirmed that the pDA coating enabled efficient coloading of both drugs via hydrogen bonding and π-π stacking, with the particle size meeting clinical requirements for TACE. The loading capacities of PCA and DOX in this codelivery system reached 4.6 and 4.8 times those of commercial microspheres, respectively. The adsorption kinetics fitted a pseudo-second-order model, and the microspheres exhibited distinct pH-responsive release behavior. In vitro and in vivo studies demonstrated that the sustained release of PCA downregulated proinflammatory cytokines, upregulated IL-10 expression, and reduced macrophage infiltration, thereby exerting analgesic and anti-inflammatory effects. Furthermore, the codelivery of PCA and DOX exerted synergistic antitumor efficacy with a coefficient of drug interaction (CDI) of 0.62-0.63, significantly inhibiting the proliferation, migration, and invasion of HCC cells. In mouse subcutaneous xenograft and rabbit VX2 liver tumor TACE models, the pDA-PVA/PCA-DOX significantly suppressed tumor growth, downregulated angiogenesis-related markers including VEGF, EGFR, and CD31, and modulated the immune microenvironment by increasing intratumoral infiltration of CD4+ and CD8+ T cells. Additionally, the pDA-PVA/PCA-DOX exhibited favorable biocompatibility and hemocompatibility, causing no persistent hepatic or renal toxicity post-TACE. Collectively, this multifunctional coloaded microsphere system integrates efficient embolization, anti-inflammation, analgesia, synergistic chemotherapy, and microenvironment modulation, providing experimental evidence for optimizing TACE therapeutic strategies.

#### [A] Running title: The plasticity of CAFs in cancer development.

- PMID: 42998866
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Clinica chimica acta; international journal of clinical chemistry
- 作者: Bakhshandeh B, Jahanafrooz Z, Ghajari G, Mohtadi D, Rahmani-Kukia N, Abbasi A, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 07
- DOI: 10.1016/j.cca.2026.122960
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42998866/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Cancer is among the leading causes of death worldwide. The tumor microenvironment is a dynamic, multifaceted milieu where the cancer cells interact with diverse components. Resident stromal cell-derived cancer-associated fibroblasts are central to the crosstalk. There is variability in the origin, phenotype, and function of CAFs, which is influenced by signaling cues from cancer cells and the extracellular matrix. The presence of CAFs in the TME coincides with tumorigenesis, invasion, metastasis, and drug resistance. Many mechanisms are involved, such as metabolic reprogramming of glucose, amino acid, and lipid metabolism. For example, enhanced lactate production can contribute to a glucose-lactate shuttle that supports tumor growth. Dysregulation of normal ECM remodeling leads to a breakdown of the basement membrane, thereby enabling tumor cells to invade. Besides contributing to poor prognosis, CAFs may induce cancer cells to create vascular-like networks. They also recruit immunosuppressive cells and inhibit the immune response. Consequently, deciphering how the different factors affecting the fate and function of CAFs signal and modulate their activities is imperative in fully grasping the complex biology of cancer to seek more effective therapeutic options. By targeting key pathways, surface markers, and conversion of CAFs to a quiescent state, the therapeutic outcome of the treatments for cancers may be improved. In this review, we summarize current knowledge on the biology of cancer-associated fibroblasts (CAFs), highlighting how distinct CAF subtypes defined by markers such as α-SMA, FAP, and MHC II (apCAFs), as well as inflammatory (iCAFs) and myofibroblastic (myCAFs) profiles-modulate extracellular matrix remodeling, metabolism, angiogenesis, immune responses, and metastatic potential. Our detailed review goes beyond explaining the potential of CAFs in developing and progressing cancer and addresses areas of research that could enable the widespread translation of this science for effective cancer therapy. Translational work and well-designed clinical trials will be required to fully transform CAFs from facilitators of resistance to actionable targets.

#### [A] Bright NIR-II fluorescence lipid membrane probes for labelling of biomimetic nanovesicles with image-guided targeted NIR-II mild photothermal-immunotherapy of orthotopic glioblastoma.

- PMID: 42762715
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate

- 作者: Huang H, Wang W, Lou Y, Yan Y, Ahmad H, Li M, et al.
- 发表日期: 2027 Mar
- DOI: 10.1016/j.biomaterials.2026.124628
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42762715/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Cell-derived nanovesicles possess the capacity to cross the blood-brain barrier (BBB) and modulate the immunosuppressive tumor microenvironment (TME); however, the lack of efficient labelling probes limits their application in tracing biodistribution and achieving synergistic glioblastoma (GBM) theranostics. Herein, we report bright NIR-II fluorescence membrane probe (CL1)-labelled nanovesicles (CL1-L-TV) composed of liposomes (L) and activated autologous T cell-derived extracellular vesicles (TV) for image-guided NIR-II mild photothermal immunotherapy of GBM. Through lipid conjugation, CL1 intercalates deeply into the L-TV bilayer, physically avoiding aggregation-caused quenching (ACQ) and achieving substantially enhanced NIR-II fluorescence quantum yield (QY = 5.98%), as confirmed by simulations, along with a high photothermal conversion efficiency (PCE = 69.1%). The overexpressed T cell receptors and PD-1 on the TV surface mediate interactions with major histocompatibility complex (MHC) and PD-L1 on GBM cells, respectively, effectively facilitating BBB crossing, GBM targeting, and immune checkpoint blockade (ICB). Furthermore, CL1-L-TV enables imaging to precisely localize GBM, reports the tumoral molecular microenvironment, mediates photothermal immunotherapy to ablate primary tumors locally, induces immunogenic cell death, releases tumor damage-associated antigens, promotes dendritic cell (DC) maturation, reinvigorates cytotoxic T lymphocyte infiltration, and elicits abscopal antitumor effects, as validated by gene expression analyses. This study highlights the potential of NIR-II fluorescent lipid probes for nanovesicle labelling and image-guided photothermal-immunotherapy.

#### [A] A dual-crosslinked silk fibroin hydrogel with aldehyde-functionalized glycyrrhizic acid as a structural-pharmacological crosslinker for diabetic wound healing.

- PMID: 42849374
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism

- 作者: Ouyang Y, Xing J, Dai M, Wang K, Yu B
- 发表日期: 2027 Feb
- DOI: 10.1016/j.bioadv.2026.215216
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42849374/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Diabetic wounds are characterized by persistent oxidative stress, dysregulate inflammation, impaired angiogenesis, and defective tissue repair. Developing wound dressings that simultaneously provide mechanical adaptability and active modulation of the pathological microenvironment remains challenging. Here, we developed a dual-crosslinked silk fibroin-based hydrogel (SCG) composed of methacrylated silk fibroin (SFGMA), oxidized carboxymethyl cellulose (OCMC), and aldehyde-functionalized glycyrrhizic acid (AGA). SFGMA formed a photocrosslinked covalent network, whereas OCMC and AGA introduced dynamic Schiff-base interactions with silk fibroin amines. Notably, AGA served as a structural-bioactive component by participating in network formation while retaining glycyrrhizic-acid-associated antioxidant and immunomodulatory activity. The SCG precursor exhibited syringe deliverability and rapid in situ photocuring, while the resulting hydrogel showed dynamic self-recovery, enhanced mechanical stability, interconnected porosity, controlled swelling and degradation, and good cyto- and hemocompatibility. In vitro, AGA-containing hydrogels markedly reduced reactive oxygen species and favored an M2-like macrophage phenotype, characterized by increased CD206, Arg-1, IL-10, and TGF-β expression together with reduced CD86, iNOS, and IL-6 expression. In LPS-stimulated macrophages, AGA-containing formulations also decreased extracellular HMGB1 levels. SCG further enhanced endothelial migration and tube formation. In a streptozotocin-induced diabetic full-thickness wound model, SCG promoted early wound-area reduction and significantly improved histological re-epithelialization, collagen matrix deposition, and vascularization, accompanied by reduced inflammatory-marker expression and increased M2-associated tissue markers. Collectively, these findings identify AGA as a promising structural-pharmacological building block for multifunctional hydrogel design and support SCG as an in situ-forming wound dressing for diabetic wound repair.

#### 摘要

Biodegradable Zn has attracted growing attention as a bone implant material because of its appropriate degradation rate and favourable mechanical properties. In this study, we used plasma electrolytic oxidation (PEO) followed by sulfurization to create a ZnO/ZnS heterostructured coating on Zn (S-PEO-Zn) for orthopedic applications. Surface characterization showed that the coating markedly increased surface roughness and improved wettability. This optimized interface effectively mitigated Zn-induced cytotoxicity and established a favourable local ionic microenvironment. Through controlled Zn ion release, S-PEO-Zn promoted macrophage polarization toward an anti-inflammatory phenotype, associated with downregulation of NF-κB-related inflammatory signalling. Furthermore, S-PEO-Zn enhanced endothelial cell migration and tube formation and upregulated angiogenesis-related gene expression in HUVECs. Meanwhile, S-PEO-Zn enhanced alkaline phosphatase activity and the expression of osteogenesis-related genes in C3H10T1/2 cells. Mouse air-pouch and rat femoral implantation models further demonstrated the favourable immunomodulatory and bone-regenerative effects of S-PEO-Zn in vivo, respectively. Overall, this work demonstrates that ZnO/ZnS-coated Zn represents a promising strategy for orthopedic applications by restoring the immune-vascular-bone coupling cascade.

- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42995172/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Accurate visualization of glioblastoma is essential for guiding biopsy, resection and radiotherapy. Contrast-enhanced MRI is routine in clinical practice, yet it can miss metabolically active tumor outside areas of blood-brain barrier disruption. In a histopathology-validated cohort with 472 tissue samples of 61 patients with IDH-wildtype glioblastoma, we show that O-(2-[¹⁸F]fluoroethyl)-L-tyrosine PET ([18F]FET PET) depicts tumor tissue significantly better than contrast-enhanced MRI (CE-MRI), especially in regions that show no enhancement on MRI. [18F]FET PET achieves substantially higher sensitivity for tumor detection (91.2% vs 52.6%) and maintains high reliability in non-enhancing tissue (positive predictive value 98.3%), findings that are consistent across newly diagnosed and recurrent disease and in both cohorts undergoing either resection or biopsy. These results indicate that [18F]FET PET complements MRI by revealing MRI-occult tumor burden. Incorporating amino acid PET into routine planning may therefore improve the accuracy of tumor visualization and, ultimately, patient management.

#### [A] Baicalein-loaded procyanidins nanoparticles alleviate neuroinflammation in Parkinson's disease by restoring microglial 8-oxoguanine DNA glycosylase 1.

- PMID: 42815649
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate

- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42815649/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-10

#### 摘要

Microglial NLRP3 inflammasome activation and M1 polarization, driven by unresolved 8-oxoguanine (8-oxoG) DNA damage resulting from downregulated 8-oxoguanine DNA glycosylase 1 (OGG1), are key drivers of neuroinflammation in Parkinson's disease (PD). Baicalein (BAI) can restore OGG1 and interrupt this pathological cycle, but its poor solubility and low brain accumulation limit its therapeutic efficacy. Procyanidins (PC), a class of flavanol polyphenols with neuroprotective and anti-inflammatory effects, can self-assemble into nanoparticles (PC NPs) to encapsulate hydrophobic BAI while exerting synergistic therapeutic effects. However, PC NPs alone fail to overcome three sequential biological barriers for PD treatment: systemic clearance, blood brain barrier (BBB) transcytosis, and selective microglial uptake. To tackle these challenges, we engineered a cascade-delivery nanoplatform named RVG/MG1-M@PC@BAI NPs for the co-delivery of BAI and PC. In this system, PC forms self-assembled inner core to encapsulate BAI. This core is further coated with an immune-evasive hybrid membrane (M) surface-functionalized with RVG29 peptide for BBB penetration and MG1 peptide for selective M1 microglial uptake. In vitro and in vivo characterization demonstrated that the nanoplatform effectively penetrates the BBB and is preferentially internalized by microglia. Pharmacological evaluation demonstrated that the nanoplatform efficiently upregulates OGG1 expression, thereby reducing 8-oxoG accumulation, suppressing aberrant NLRP3 inflammasome activation, restoring the physiological balance of microglial M1/M2 phenotypes, and ultimately alleviating neuroinflammation. Safety assessment confirmed its favorable biocompatibility. In conclusion, RVG/MG1-M@PC@BAI NPs represent an OGG1-targeted synergistic nanoplatform, providing a promising strategy for PD therapy.

#### [A] Conus medullaris-cauda arteriovenous malformation and Klippel-Trenaunay syndrome: what is the treatment goal?

#### 摘要

A 29-year-old man with Klippel-Trenaunay syndrome (KTS) presented with a symptomatic conus medullaris-cauda arteriovenous malformation (AVM) manifesting as back and right limb pain, which abruptly worsened with the onset of right limb weakness and urinary retention. He was treated by multisession endovascular embolization resulting in improved neurological status. KTS is a sporadic disease with unknown etiology, but genetic susceptibility may lead to the over-expression of angiogenic factors and increased angiogenesis. KTS may be exceptionally associated with slow-flow spinal AVM, but there is no consensus about the optimal treatment for these symptomatic lesions. Embolization treatment may represent a safe option to minimize complications and possibly improve the neurological status in patients with spinal AVM associated with KTS, if one or both legs are already impaired by hypertrophy or other vascular malformations. Genetic analysis may reveal an underlying angiogenesis change, so closer follow up might be indicated in selected patients.

#### [A] Disulfidptosis-Driven Immune-Activated Multimodal Therapy for Glioblastoma with Systemic Safety Control.

#### 摘要

Effective glioblastoma therapy requires simultaneous solutions to poor blood-brain barrier (BBB) penetration, insufficient tumor-selective killing, and treatment-related systemic toxicity. We hypothesized that a BBB-penetrating nanoplatform capable of generating distinct H2S exposures in tumor and normal tissues could enhance antitumor efficacy while mitigating doxorubicin (DOX)-induced cardiotoxicity. Here, DOX-loaded metastable molybdenum disulfide nanoflowers functionalized with an I6P8 peptide (DOX@MSI) were developed. After BBB transport and tumor accumulation, the acidic, H2O2-rich, and GSH-rich microenvironment accelerates nanoplatform degradation, promoting GSH depletion and enhanced H2S/DOX release. High local H2S upregulates xCT, while H2S/DOX-mediated mitochondrial inhibition suppresses NADPH regeneration, synergistically triggering disulfidptosis. Meanwhile, H2S suppresses the JAK2/STAT3/IDO axis, alleviating DOX-induced immunosuppression and enhancing immunogenic cell death and T-cell-mediated antitumor immunity. In contrast, slower degradation under physiological conditions enables sustained low-level H2S release, reducing the level of oxidative stress and ERK-associated apoptosis in cardiomyocytes. Together with NIR-enhanced photothermal therapy, DOX@MSI suppresses orthotopic glioblastoma while reducing cardiac injury, thereby integrating tumor-selective multimodal therapy with active organ protection.

#### [A] Diagnostic and prognostic biomarkers for pediatric cerebral malaria: a review.

- PMID: 43007515
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Molecular biology reports
- 作者: Drelichowska A, Pikor D, Hurla M, Banaszek-Hurla N, Karnicka M, Błaszków K, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 10
- DOI: 10.1007/s11033-026-13115-9
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43007515/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-11

#### 摘要

Pediatric cerebral malaria (PCM) kills 15% to 25% of the African children in whom it is diagnosed and leaves many survivors with neurocognitive deficits. It is a disease of the cerebral microvasculature whose pathogenic sequence is now partly resolved at the molecular level. Plasmodium falciparum erythrocyte membrane protein 1 binds a broad range of endothelial cell receptors, of which binding to the endothelial protein C receptor, in some cases together with intercellular adhesion molecule 1, is the combination linked to severe and cerebral disease. CD36 is the receptor most commonly used by field isolates but is associated with uncomplicated malaria, at least two dozen further receptors have been described for parasitized erythrocytes, and a PfEMP1 binding domain has been identified for only a few of them. The ensuing endothelial activation releases von Willebrand factor and angiopoietin-2 and disables Tie - 2 signaling. Free heme, delivered in part by histidine-rich protein 2 nanoparticles internalized by caveolin-1-dependent endocytosis, is proposed to engage two separable innate arms, one through stimulator of interferon genes 1 (STING1) yielding type I interferon and CXCL10, the other through NLRP3 and caspase - 1 yielding interleukin-1-beta; the first was shown in murine brain endothelium and the second in a human cerebral endothelial cell line, and the two have not been demonstrated in one system. Junctional remodeling and ephrin type-A receptor 2 signaling open the blood-brain barrier in murine models, barrier disruption is documented histologically in human autopsy series, and neuronal and axonal proteins reach the circulation of affected children. The sequence summarized here therefore combines links established in human disease with links that remain experimental or proposed, and the review marks which is which at each step. This review treats candidate biomarkers as readouts of that cascade rather than as free-standing correlates. Because the clinical definition cannot separate malaria-attributable coma from coma with incidental parasitemia, we appraise malarial retinopathy as the in vivo reference standard and set out why a negative result is heterogeneous. Parasite-derived and host-derived markers are mapped onto those steps, with attention to the few evaluated against non-malarial coma rather than against milder malaria, and to what a marker adds at the bedside. Extracellular vesicles and microRNAs are assessed as emerging platforms. We close on druggable nodes in the cascade and on using molecular readouts to stratify children for host-directed therapy.

- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 10
- DOI: 10.1177/10849785261490722
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43004914/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-11

#### 摘要

Targeted radiopharmaceutical therapy has entered mainstream oncology through the clinical success of prostate-specific membrane antigen (PSMA)-directed imaging and [177Lu] Lu-PSMA-617 therapy. However, heterogeneous antigen expression, acquired resistance, lineage plasticity, and nonresponding lesions show that a cancer cell-centric strategy cannot fully address the ecological complexity of advanced malignancy. The tumor microenvironment (TME) offers a complementary target space because stromal, vascular, immune, and chemokine-driven niches are functionally required for tumor growth and are often more genomically stable than malignant cells. This review synthesizes the biological rationale, radiochemical design, clinical translation, and current limitations of TME-targeted radiopharmaceuticals, focusing on fibroblast activation protein (FAP), integrins, and the CXCR4/CXCL12 axis. The authors discuss first-generation FAP inhibitor (FAPI) tracers and therapeutic limitations caused by short tumor residence, and then compare next-generation strategies, including dimerization, multimerization, albumin-binding motifs, peptide-based ligands such as FAP-2286, and newer FAPI-derived therapeutic constructs. They also reassess RGD-based integrin targeting as a clinically informative but heterogeneous angiogenesis platform, and summarize CXCR4-directed pentixather therapy in hematological malignancies and selected solid tumor models. Finally, they examine radionuclide selection, dosimetry, patient selection, and combination strategies with chemotherapy, antiangiogenic therapy, immune checkpoint blockade, cytokine therapy, and cell-based therapy. TME-targeted radiopharmaceuticals are unlikely to replace tumor cell-directed theranostics; rather, their most important role may be to reshape resistant tumor ecosystems and rationally extend precision radionuclide therapy beyond single-antigen paradigms.

#### [A] Targeting Systemic Immune Dysregulation in Cardiovascular Disease: From Mechanisms to Precision Therapeutic Strategies.

- PMID: 43009736
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Cardiovascular drugs and therapy
- 作者: Li H, Ou Y, Zhu X, Fan G, Li L
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 11
- DOI: 10.1007/s10557-026-07985-1
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43009736/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-12

#### 摘要

Cardiovascular diseases remain a leading cause of morbidity and mortality despite advances in conventional pharmacological therapy. Persistent inflammation and immune dysregulation contribute to residual cardiovascular risk, disease heterogeneity, and variable therapeutic responses. Increasing evidence links cardiovascular injury to systemic immune and immunometabolic disturbances arising from the kidney, liver, brain, hematopoietic and lymphoid organs, gut, and adipose tissue. This review summarizes how systemic immune disturbances contribute to cardiovascular remodeling through altered immune-cell activation, impaired inflammatory resolution, metabolic stress, and pathological immune memory. We discuss the contributions of kidney-, liver-, neuroimmune-, gut-adipose-, and hematopoietic-associated abnormalities to major cardiovascular phenotypes, including heart failure with preserved ejection fraction, atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease, cardiorenal syndrome, metabolic cardiomyopathy, and aging-associated cardiovascular disease. These systemic abnormalities converge on common cardiovascular effector mechanisms, including endothelial dysfunction, inflammatory-cell recruitment, macrophage activation, fibroblast remodeling, extracellular matrix deposition, and cardiomyocyte dysfunction. We further examine emerging pharmacological strategies targeting systemic immune dysregulation, including cytokine inhibition, inflammasome modulation, metabolic interventions, and therapies targeting maladaptive immune memory. Although several approaches have demonstrated clinical benefit, effective translation requires identification of biologically defined patient subgroups, reliable biomarkers, and appropriate assessment of therapeutic response and safety. Integrating immune profiling with cardiovascular pharmacotherapy may facilitate a transition from nonspecific anti-inflammatory approaches toward precision modulation of pathological immune networks.

#### [A] Temporal Cascade of Post-Stroke Cognitive Impairment: Metabolic-Mitochondrial Crosstalk.

- PMID: 43009658
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Molecular neurobiology
- 作者: Li Y, Wu Y, Han R, Luo N, Tian Y, Zhao B, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 11
- DOI: 10.1007/s12035-026-06308-7
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43009658/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-12

#### 摘要

Post-stroke cognitive impairment (PSCI) is a common and highly disabling complication of stroke. Current research on its molecular mechanisms has largely focused on neuroinflammatory cascades and blood-brain barrier disruption caused by microglial polarization, while the role of cellular metabolism remains underexplored. Metabolic reprogramming is a coordinated network-level adjustment of carbohydrate, lipid, and amino acid metabolism, and has recently become a research hotspot in neurological diseases, particularly centered on phenotypic switching of microglia following metabolic reprogramming. As the center of cellular energy metabolism, mitochondria are closely linked to normal cellular bioenergetics through biogenesis, motility, and quality control; disruption of any of these links after brain injury is intimately associated with neuronal activity. In this review, we adopt a metabolism-oriented perspective to explore the changes in the brain microenvironment after metabolic reprogramming, connect the critical role of mitochondrial dysfunction in disease development, and deeply analyze the core positions of both in the pathogenesis of PSCI, presenting the complex regulatory network linking metabolic disturbances and mitochondrial dysfunction, as described in the Graphical Abstract. Furthermore, we discuss the future prospects of emerging technologies such as mitochondrial transplantation and stem cell therapy, aiming to translate theoretical advances into clinical applications for PSCI.

#### [A] Glycosaminoglycan-Induced Macrophage Reprogramming Orchestrates Pro-Regenerative Crosstalk with Fibroblasts.

- PMID: 43009078
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Acta biomaterialia
- 作者: Du Q, Mace K, Wong KFC, Lawler K, Kotowska A, AlRomaihi RA, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 10
- DOI: 10.1016/j.actbio.2026.11.011
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43009078/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-12

#### 摘要

Macrophages and fibroblasts play central roles in coordinating inflammation and tissue repair, whilst dysregulation of macrophage-fibroblast crosstalk drives pathological outcomes such as chronic inflammation and fibrosis. Glycosaminoglycans (GAGs), key components of the extracellular matrix (ECM), interact with cytokines and growth factors that modulate macrophage polarisation and direct fibroblast activity, making them attractive candidates for developing immune-instructive biomaterials. However, the optimal GAG compositions that promote macrophage-mediated repair and fibroblast-driven ECM remodelling remain unclear. Here, we report an unbiased screening of 82 surface-adsorbed GAG combinations to identify candidates capable of modulating macrophage-fibroblast interactions. Leveraging cytokine profiling, surface marker immunostaining, gene expression, principal component analysis (PCA), and hierarchical clustering, we identified 'hit' GAG combinations that induced distinct, non-canonical macrophage activation programmes characterised by coordinated inflammatory and regulatory signalling, alongside enhanced fibroblast proliferation. Shortlisted formulations were validated using expanded biological replication, orthogonal phenotypic, transcriptional and functional analyses, assessment in macrophage-fibroblast co-culture systems, and in an in vivo diabetic wound model. These data demonstrated that 'hit' GAG formulations modulated macrophage functional activation states, promoted collagen and ECM synthesis, facilitated vascular and lymphatic vessel formation, and accelerated wound closure. Complementary metabolomic profiling using OrbiSIMS and molecular formula prediction (MFP), revealed macrophage metabolic reprogramming as a potential mechanism underlying these pro-healing effects. Collectively, this study established an unbiased methodology for discovering immune-instructive GAGs in supporting pro-healing responses for potential wound-healing applications. STATEMENT OF SIGNIFICANCE: Glycosaminoglycans (GAGs) are key extracellular matrix components with significant potential to regulate immune responses. However, their immunomodulatory effects are highly context-dependent, varying with sulfation pattern, molecular composition, and the local microenvironment, making it challenging to identify GAG combinations that consistently promote pro-regenerative immune responses and tissue repair. Here, we developed an unbiased combinatorial screening platform to systematically identify GAG combinations that reprogram macrophages toward pro-regenerative functional states. The selected GAG combinations enhanced macrophage-fibroblast crosstalk in vitro and improved diabetic wound healing in vivo by promoting collagen deposition, angiogenesis, and lymphatic remodelling. OrbiSIMS metabolomics further revealed macrophage metabolic reprogramming associated with these regenerative responses. This work establishes a generalizable discovery framework for identifying immune-regulatory GAG formulations and provides new design principles for developing extracellular matrix-inspired immunomodulatory biomaterials for regenerative medicine.

#### [A] An overview of clinical and experimental treatment modalities for port wine stains.

- DOI: 10.1016/j.jaad.2011.11.938
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22305042/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-13

#### 摘要

Port wine stains (PWS) are the most common vascular malformation of the skin, occurring in 0.3% to 0.5% of the population. Noninvasive laser irradiation with flashlamp-pumped pulsed dye lasers (selective photothermolysis) currently comprises the gold standard treatment of PWS; however, the majority of PWS fail to clear completely after selective photothermolysis. In this review, the clinically used PWS treatment modalities (pulsed dye lasers, alexandrite lasers, neodymium:yttrium-aluminum-garnet lasers, and intense pulsed light) and techniques (combination approaches, multiple passes, and epidermal cooling) are discussed. Retrospective analysis of clinical studies published between 1990 and 2011 was performed to determine therapeutic efficacies for each clinically used modality/technique. In addition, factors that have resulted in the high degree of therapeutic recalcitrance are identified, and emerging experimental treatment strategies are addressed, including the use of photodynamic therapy, immunomodulators, angiogenesis inhibitors, hypobaric pressure, and site-specific pharmaco-laser therapy.

#### [A] Hyperbaric oxygen therapy: can it prevent irradiation-induced necrosis?

- PMID: 22465460
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 静脉畸形基础与治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Experimental neurology
- 作者: Kuffler DP
- 发表日期: 2012 Jun
- DOI: 10.1016/j.expneurol.2012.03.011
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22465460/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Radiosurgery is an important non-invasive procedure for the treatment of tumors and vascular malformations. However, in addition to killing target tissues, cranial irradiation induces damage to adjacent healthy tissues leading to neurological deterioration in both pediatric and adult patients, which is poorly understood and insufficiently treatable. To minimize irradiation damage to healthy tissue, not the optimal therapeutic irradiation dose required to eliminate the target lesion is used but lower doses. Although the success rate of irradiation surgery is about 95%, 5% of patients suffer problems, most commonly neurological, that are thought to be a direct consequence of irradiation-induced inflammation. Although no direct correlation has been demonstrated, the appearance and disappearance of inflammation that develops following irradiation commonly parallel the appearance and disappearance of neurological side effects that are associated with the neurological function of the irradiated brain regions. These observations have led to the hypothesis that brain inflammation is causally related to the observed neurological side effects. Studies indicate that hyperbaric oxygen therapy (HBOT) applied after the appearance of irradiation-induced neurological side effects reduces the incidence and severity of those side effects. This may result from HBOT reducing inflammation, promoting angiogenesis, and influencing other cellular functions thereby suppressing events that cause the neurological side effects. However, it would be significantly better for the patient if rather than waiting for neurological side effects to become manifest they could be avoided. This review examines irradiation-induced neurological side effects, methods that minimize or resolve those side effects, and concludes with a discussion of whether HBOT applied following irradiation, but before manifestation of neurological side effects may prevent or reduce the appearance of irradiation-induced neurological side effects.

#### [A] PPARγ at the Nexus of Lipid Metabolism and Neuroinflammation in Alzheimer's Disease: A Longitudinal Multi-omics Analysis.

- PMID: 43024124
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Journal of molecular neuroscience : MN
- 作者: Huixuan H, Ruizhe W, Chunrong H, Na W, Tianwai D, Ludan Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 13
- DOI: 10.1007/s12031-026-02609-7
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43024124/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Peroxisome proliferator-activated receptor gamma (PPARγ) has emerged as an important regulator linking lipid metabolism and neuroimmune responses in Alzheimer's disease (AD). Increasing evidence suggests that disruption of the lipid-immune axis contributes to disease progression and may underlie the heterogeneous therapeutic responses observed with PPARγ-targeted interventions. Experimental studies indicate that PPARγ activation influences multiple biological processes relevant to AD, including lipid homeostasis, mitochondrial function, inflammatory signaling, and amyloid-β clearance. However, the translation of these findings into consistent clinical benefit remains limited by challenges such as poor blood-brain barrier penetration, systemic adverse effects, and disease stage-dependent therapeutic responses. Recent advances in multi-omics technologies and single-cell transcriptomics have provided new insights into the context-dependent roles of PPARγ in different neural cell populations, highlighting dynamic interactions between lipid metabolism, neuroinflammation, and cellular function throughout AD progression. These findings suggest that PPARγ-associated pathways may represent an important regulatory interface linking metabolic and inflammatory processes, while emphasizing the complexity of their temporal and cell-specific regulation. This review synthesizes current evidence from experimental studies, human multi-omics investigations, and clinical research to provide an updated perspective on the role of PPARγ in coordinating lipid metabolism and neuroinflammation in AD. In addition, we discuss the major translational challenges and emerging therapeutic strategies, including selective PPARγ modulation, improved central nervous system delivery, and precision treatment approaches tailored to disease stage.

#### [A] Stem cell therapy attenuates cerebellar histological alterations and blood-brain barrier disruption in experimental fibromyalgia: involvement of mast cells, autophagy, and NLRP3/IL-1β neuroinflammatory pathway.

- PMID: 43021681
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate

- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism

- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Tissue barriers
- 作者: Mokhemer SA, Abdelghany AK, Hassan SM, AbdelHaleem AH
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 13
- DOI: 10.1080/21688370.2026.2762719
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43021681/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Fibromyalgia is a prevalent and debilitating condition that results in generalized musculoskeletal pain, trouble sleeping, exhaustion, psychological challenges, and social problems. As a consequence of fibromyalgia's substantial therapeutic and socioeconomic burden caused by its high prevalence, uncertain etiology, wide spectrum of clinical manifestations, and sophisticated therapy, a considerable interest has emerged in investigating novel aspects of its pathophysiology, particularly those related to anxiety, depression, and cerebellar histological alterations. Such investigations may contribute to the development of effective therapeutic strategies, including the use of bone marrow-derived mesenchymal stem cells (BMSCs) as a promising treatment approach. Rats were randomly divided into three groups: control, fibromyalgia and fibromyalgia-stem cell groups. Behavioral assessment was carried out. Cerebellar cortices were collected for histological, immunohistochemical, and morphometric analysis. Fibromyalgia group revealed anxiety-like behavioral changes, and neurodegenerative changes with blood-brain barrier (BBB) affection. Furthermore, NLRP3 and IL-1β immune-expression were significantly increased, with significant increase in the total number of mast cells with marked degranulation. Additionally, there was a significant decrease in Calbindin D-28k (CB), Beclin-1 and LC3 immune-expression. Fibromyalgia-stem cells group revealed amelioration in behavioral alterations, histological improvement, significant decrease in CD117, NLRP3 and IL-1β immune-expression, besides a significant increase in CB, Beclin-1 and LC3 immune-expression. This study represents one of the first experimental studies investigating the potential therapeutic value of BMSCs therapy in fibromyalgia-related cerebellar damage through neuroprotective mechanisms involving enhancement of autophagy, mast cell deactivation, and suppression of NLRP3/IL-1β mediated neuroinflammation with improvement of BBB structural integrity.

#### [A] Microglial Metabolic Reprogramming Regulated by HIF-1α/HK2 Positive Feedback Inhibits Adult Hippocampal Neurogenesis in Diabetes-Associated Cognitive Dysfunction.

- PMID: 43020960
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Molecular neurobiology
- 作者: Jiang S, Zhao Y, Zhang Q, Zhang J, Gao Z, Luo Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 13
- DOI: 10.1007/s12035-026-06348-z
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43020960/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Diabetes-associated cognitive dysfunction (DACD) is a significant complication with few effective treatments and unclear underlying mechanism. The aim of this study was to investigate how hyperglycemia disrupts adult hippocampal neurogenesis (AHN) through metabolic-immune interactions, seeking to identify potential treatment targets for DACD. DACD models were developed using db/db mice or streptozotocin-induced mice, and their cognitive function was assessed through behavioral tests. Next, we integrated multi-omics analyses (transcriptomic, single-cell, and lactylome profiling) with metabolic studies in BV2 cells exposed to diabetic conditions. Pharmacological modulators (3-BP, C646, TSA) were used to clarify the underlying mechanisms. The mice with DACD demonstrated cognitive deficits, impaired AHN, and activation of hippocampal microglia. Transcriptomic analysis revealed glycolysis as the primary dysregulated pathway. Mechanistically, hyperglycemia/AGEs induced glycolytic reprogramming and increased lactate levels, which promoted HIF-1α lactylation at lysine residues-a modification catalyzed by p300 and opposed by histone deacetylases. This posttranslational modification was associated with HIF-1α stabilisation and with enhanced transcriptional occupancy at the HK2 promoter. This established a self-amplifying feedback loop, perpetuating microglial activation and disrupting the blood-brain barrier via CXCL10 secretion, which led to CD8+ T-cell infiltration. Systemic administration of the glycolytic inhibitor 3-BP disrupted the pathogenic circuit, thereby normalizing metabolism, attenuating neuroinflammation, and restoring cognitive function. HIF-1α lactylation is associated with HIF-1α stabilisation, acting as a potential metabolic-epigenetic mechanism that links glycolytic flux to sustained microglial activation, neuroinflammation and impaired AHN in DACD. Pharmacological inhibition of this axis attenuated neuroinflammation and improved cognitive outcomes in our model, suggesting a candidate therapeutic strategy for DACD.

#### [A] EXPRESS: Oligodendrocyte precursor cells in ischemic stroke: from biological properties and glial interactions to therapeutic implications.

- PMID: 43021509
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗

- 作者: Wang X, Ding Z, Song Y, Wu Z, Yu W, Zhao P, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 12
- DOI: 10.1177/0271678X261508669
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43021509/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Ischemic stroke remains a leading cause of mortality and long-term disability worldwide. Although revascularization therapies have improved outcomes in certain patients, effective interventions for neurological deficits are still lacking. White matter injury plays a central role in driving persistent functional impairments. As the cellular foundation for myelin regeneration, oligodendrocyte precursor cells (OPCs) have emerged as a promising therapeutic target. This review systematically examines the pathophysiological alterations of OPCs following ischemic stroke, including their proliferation, migration, differentiation, and maturation into myelinating oligodendrocytes, and explores their dynamic interactions with the surrounding microenvironment, including microglia, astrocytes, neurons, and endothelial cells. Accumulating evidence indicates that OPCs not only facilitate myelin repair through differentiation into oligodendrocytes but also contribute to neuroprotection and vascular remodeling by secreting neurotrophic and pro-angiogenic factors. Therapeutic strategies aimed at preserving OPCs and enhancing their differentiation, including pharmacological therapy, cell transplantation, gene therapy, bioengineering and biomaterials technology, as well as rehabilitation and physical therapy, hold considerable promise for improving neurological recovery. By positioning OPC dysfunction as a driver of persistent disability, this review outlines a translational pathway toward therapies that restore white matter integrity and functional recovery after ischemic stroke.

#### [A] The blood-brain barrier imports diverse neuroprotective proteins through pathways impaired early in Alzheimer's disease models.

- PMID: 43014307
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate

- 作者: Zhou H, Maynard JC, Pennacchio AJ, Apolonio A, Liu X, Yao L, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 11
- DOI: 10.1016/j.cell.2026.10.039
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43014307/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

The blood-brain barrier (BBB) has been defined primarily by what it excludes, yet the scale and function of physiological blood-to-brain protein transport remain largely unknown. Using bioorthogonal proteomics, we find that the healthy BBB samples 191 plasma proteins and permits 92 into the brain parenchyma. Enriched for carrier and metabolic functions, these proteins home to distinct brain cell types, such as apolipoprotein A1 (APOA1) to astrocytes and alpha-1 antitrypsin (SERPINA1) to microglia. This cell-type tropism relies on a BBB-parenchyma receptor hand-off: APOA1 crosses the BBB via SR-B1 and engages astrocytic ATP-binding cassette transporter A1 (ABCA1), a key Alzheimer's risk gene, driving cholesterol efflux that supports synapse integrity. Unlike LPS-induced nonspecific leakage, early-stage 5xFAD mice show selective BBB transport remodeling, with reduced APOA1 and increased SERPINA1 uptake. Hepatic APOA1 overexpression restores APOA1 in the 5xFAD brain, rescuing astrocytic, synaptic, and behavioral deficits. These findings recast the BBB as a selective interface that recruits neuroprotective circulatory proteins for brain health.

#### [A] USP28 potentiates endothelial necroptosis via deubiquitinating NMMHC IIA in septic male mice.

- PMID: 43020782
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology

- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism

- 主题: 跨领域顶刊启发

- 期刊: Nature communications
- 作者: Tang J, Hu D, Xu M, Chen H, Gong S, Li F, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 15
- DOI: 10.1038/s41467-026-78666-8
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43020782/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Endothelial dysfunction contributes to multi-organ injury in sepsis, but the underlying mechanisms remain incompletely understood. Here we show that endothelial ubiquitin-specific protease 28 (USP28) is increased in patients with sepsis and in male mice in a murine polymicrobial sepsis model. Mechanistically, USP28 stabilizes non-muscle myosin heavy chain IIA (NMMHC IIA) by removing K48-linked ubiquitination at K1413. Moreover, NMMHC IIA promotes interferon-α/β receptor 1 (IFNAR1) trafficking to the plasma membrane, enhancing IFNAR1/signal transducer and activator of transcription 1 (STAT1) signaling and endothelial necroptosis. In turn, STAT1 transcriptionally increases USP28 expression, forming a positive feedback loop. Endothelial USP28 deletion or pharmacological inhibition attenuates sepsis-induced multi-organ injury, whereas USP28 overexpression exacerbates it. Conversely, endothelial NMMHC IIA or IFNAR1 overexpression largely abrogates the protection conferred by USP28 deficiency in septic male mice. These findings identify a USP28-NMMHC IIA axis that promotes endothelial necroptosis and multi-organ injury in sepsis through IFNAR1/STAT1 signaling.

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Brain arteriovenous malformations (BAVMs) are formed by hypertrophied arterial vessels (afferents, feeders), a large number of arteriovenous shunts which become tangled to form a body (nidus) of malformation, which then expands draining proximal veins. The aim of this study was a replication of single nucleotide polymorphism (SNP) rs11672433 association with BAVM development with the subsequent meta-analysis of published data.
METHODS: A total of 252 Russian patients with brain BAVMs and 480 control subjects were included in the present study. Genotyping was performed using real-time polymerase chain reaction with competitive hydrolysis probes.
RESULTS: In our case-control study, we found no significant association with brain arteriovenous malformation for the SNP rs11672433 of ANGPTL4 gene (odds ratio .82, 95% confidence interval = .57-1.17 P value = .27) as well as in meta-analysis (odds ratio 1.18, 95% confidence interval = .81-1.73, P value = .39).
CONCLUSIONS: Our data showed that SNP rs11672433 was not associated with the BAVM Russian population and the following meta-analysis did not detect an association in total. Thus, in spite of the fact that ANGPTL4 (protein) participates in the angiogenesis regulation processes, we consider that SNP rs11672433, a high-frequency locus in the ANGPTL4 gene, does not influence the predisposition to BAVM or its effect is too small to be detected in the present size sample set.

#### [A] Argatroban mitigates Klebsiella pneumoniae liver injury by attenuating ZBP1-dependent PANoptosis.

- PMID: 42753327
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: International immunopharmacology
- 作者: Wang H, Chen L, Wang H, Chang Z
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 15
- DOI: 10.1016/j.intimp.2026.117429
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42753327/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-15

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Klebsiella pneumoniae (K. pneumoniae) liver abscess (KPLA) is frequently accompanied by thrombophlebitis and systemic inflammation. Argatroban, a direct thrombin inhibitor, has anti-inflammatory potential, but its efficacy and mechanism in KPLA remain unclear.
METHODS: A murine KPLA model was established using a hypervirulent, patient-derived K. pneumoniae strain, with mice receiving vehicle or argatroban (10 mg kg-1/day, i.p.). Survival, liver pathology, vascular permeability, inflammatory responses, and transcriptomic changes were evaluated. Hepatocyte responses were investigated using KP-LPS-stimulated THLE-2 cells. ZBP1 localization, protein interactions, and functional dependence were assessed by immunofluorescence, co-immunoprecipitation, and ZBP1 silencing.
RESULTS: Argatroban significantly improved 72-h survival (P = 0.02), reduced hepatic necrosis, attenuated sinusoidal vascular leakage, and suppressed systemic inflammatory cytokine production in KPLA mice. RNA-seq revealed activation of innate immune-sensing pathways during infection, which were markedly attenuated by argatroban. ZBP1 and PANoptosis-associated molecules were increased in KPLA livers and reduced by argatroban. ZBP1 was predominantly localized in hepatocytes. In KP-LPS-stimulated THLE-2 cells, RIPK3, ASC, and Caspase-8 showed enhanced intracellular co-localization, while co-immunoprecipitation demonstrated ZBP1 interactions with RIPK3, ASC, and Caspase-8. ZBP1 silencing markedly reduced KP-LPS-induced activation of p-MLKL, cleaved GSDMD, and cleaved caspase-3, supporting ZBP1-dependent PANoptosis as a key driver of hepatocyte inflammatory injury. Mechanistically, argatroban inhibited TLR4 endocytosis and downstream TBK1/IRF3-STAT1 signaling, thereby suppressing ZBP1 induction.
CONCLUSIONS: Argatroban protects against KPLA-associated liver injury by inhibiting a TLR4 endocytosis-TBK1/IRF3-STAT1-ZBP1 axis and suppressing ZBP1-dependent PANoptosis, identifying PANoptosis as a potential therapeutic target in severe K. pneumoniae infection.

#### [A] Procaine and Doxorubicin Coloaded Microspheres for Painless and Potent Transarterial Chemoembolization.

- PMID: 42994449
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: ACS applied materials & interfaces
- 作者: Tian C, Wang W, Cai D, Zhang H, Li X, Liu S, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 09
- DOI: 10.1021/acsami.6c15393
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42994449/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Transarterial chemoembolization (TACE) is the standard treatment for intermediate-stage hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC). However, its clinical efficacy is severely limited by postembolization syndrome (PES) and the physical vascular barrier induced by embolization. To address these limitations, we developed multifunctional polyvinyl alcohol embolization microspheres modified with polydopamine and coloaded with procaine (PCA) and doxorubicin (DOX), termed pDA-PVA/PCA-DOX. MD/MM-PBSA simulations and physicochemical characterization confirmed that the pDA coating enabled efficient coloading of both drugs via hydrogen bonding and π-π stacking, with the particle size meeting clinical requirements for TACE. The loading capacities of PCA and DOX in this codelivery system reached 4.6 and 4.8 times those of commercial microspheres, respectively. The adsorption kinetics fitted a pseudo-second-order model, and the microspheres exhibited distinct pH-responsive release behavior. In vitro and in vivo studies demonstrated that the sustained release of PCA downregulated proinflammatory cytokines, upregulated IL-10 expression, and reduced macrophage infiltration, thereby exerting analgesic and anti-inflammatory effects. Furthermore, the codelivery of PCA and DOX exerted synergistic antitumor efficacy with a coefficient of drug interaction (CDI) of 0.62-0.63, significantly inhibiting the proliferation, migration, and invasion of HCC cells. In mouse subcutaneous xenograft and rabbit VX2 liver tumor TACE models, the pDA-PVA/PCA-DOX significantly suppressed tumor growth, downregulated angiogenesis-related markers including VEGF, EGFR, and CD31, and modulated the immune microenvironment by increasing intratumoral infiltration of CD4+ and CD8+ T cells. Additionally, the pDA-PVA/PCA-DOX exhibited favorable biocompatibility and hemocompatibility, causing no persistent hepatic or renal toxicity post-TACE. Collectively, this multifunctional coloaded microsphere system integrates efficient embolization, anti-inflammation, analgesia, synergistic chemotherapy, and microenvironment modulation, providing experimental evidence for optimizing TACE therapeutic strategies.

#### [A] Running title: The plasticity of CAFs in cancer development.

- PMID: 42998866
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Clinica chimica acta; international journal of clinical chemistry
- 作者: Bakhshandeh B, Jahanafrooz Z, Ghajari G, Mohtadi D, Rahmani-Kukia N, Abbasi A, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 07
- DOI: 10.1016/j.cca.2026.122960
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42998866/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Cancer is among the leading causes of death worldwide. The tumor microenvironment is a dynamic, multifaceted milieu where the cancer cells interact with diverse components. Resident stromal cell-derived cancer-associated fibroblasts are central to the crosstalk. There is variability in the origin, phenotype, and function of CAFs, which is influenced by signaling cues from cancer cells and the extracellular matrix. The presence of CAFs in the TME coincides with tumorigenesis, invasion, metastasis, and drug resistance. Many mechanisms are involved, such as metabolic reprogramming of glucose, amino acid, and lipid metabolism. For example, enhanced lactate production can contribute to a glucose-lactate shuttle that supports tumor growth. Dysregulation of normal ECM remodeling leads to a breakdown of the basement membrane, thereby enabling tumor cells to invade. Besides contributing to poor prognosis, CAFs may induce cancer cells to create vascular-like networks. They also recruit immunosuppressive cells and inhibit the immune response. Consequently, deciphering how the different factors affecting the fate and function of CAFs signal and modulate their activities is imperative in fully grasping the complex biology of cancer to seek more effective therapeutic options. By targeting key pathways, surface markers, and conversion of CAFs to a quiescent state, the therapeutic outcome of the treatments for cancers may be improved. In this review, we summarize current knowledge on the biology of cancer-associated fibroblasts (CAFs), highlighting how distinct CAF subtypes defined by markers such as α-SMA, FAP, and MHC II (apCAFs), as well as inflammatory (iCAFs) and myofibroblastic (myCAFs) profiles-modulate extracellular matrix remodeling, metabolism, angiogenesis, immune responses, and metastatic potential. Our detailed review goes beyond explaining the potential of CAFs in developing and progressing cancer and addresses areas of research that could enable the widespread translation of this science for effective cancer therapy. Translational work and well-designed clinical trials will be required to fully transform CAFs from facilitators of resistance to actionable targets.

#### [A] Bright NIR-II fluorescence lipid membrane probes for labelling of biomimetic nanovesicles with image-guided targeted NIR-II mild photothermal-immunotherapy of orthotopic glioblastoma.

- PMID: 42762715
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Biomaterials
- 作者: Huang H, Wang W, Lou Y, Yan Y, Ahmad H, Li M, et al.
- 发表日期: 2027 Mar
- DOI: 10.1016/j.biomaterials.2026.124628
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42762715/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Cell-derived nanovesicles possess the capacity to cross the blood-brain barrier (BBB) and modulate the immunosuppressive tumor microenvironment (TME); however, the lack of efficient labelling probes limits their application in tracing biodistribution and achieving synergistic glioblastoma (GBM) theranostics. Herein, we report bright NIR-II fluorescence membrane probe (CL1)-labelled nanovesicles (CL1-L-TV) composed of liposomes (L) and activated autologous T cell-derived extracellular vesicles (TV) for image-guided NIR-II mild photothermal immunotherapy of GBM. Through lipid conjugation, CL1 intercalates deeply into the L-TV bilayer, physically avoiding aggregation-caused quenching (ACQ) and achieving substantially enhanced NIR-II fluorescence quantum yield (QY = 5.98%), as confirmed by simulations, along with a high photothermal conversion efficiency (PCE = 69.1%). The overexpressed T cell receptors and PD-1 on the TV surface mediate interactions with major histocompatibility complex (MHC) and PD-L1 on GBM cells, respectively, effectively facilitating BBB crossing, GBM targeting, and immune checkpoint blockade (ICB). Furthermore, CL1-L-TV enables imaging to precisely localize GBM, reports the tumoral molecular microenvironment, mediates photothermal immunotherapy to ablate primary tumors locally, induces immunogenic cell death, releases tumor damage-associated antigens, promotes dendritic cell (DC) maturation, reinvigorates cytotoxic T lymphocyte infiltration, and elicits abscopal antitumor effects, as validated by gene expression analyses. This study highlights the potential of NIR-II fluorescent lipid probes for nanovesicle labelling and image-guided photothermal-immunotherapy.

#### [A] A dual-crosslinked silk fibroin hydrogel with aldehyde-functionalized glycyrrhizic acid as a structural-pharmacological crosslinker for diabetic wound healing.

- PMID: 42849374
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Biomaterials advances
- 作者: Ouyang Y, Xing J, Dai M, Wang K, Yu B
- 发表日期: 2027 Feb
- DOI: 10.1016/j.bioadv.2026.215216
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42849374/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Diabetic wounds are characterized by persistent oxidative stress, dysregulate inflammation, impaired angiogenesis, and defective tissue repair. Developing wound dressings that simultaneously provide mechanical adaptability and active modulation of the pathological microenvironment remains challenging. Here, we developed a dual-crosslinked silk fibroin-based hydrogel (SCG) composed of methacrylated silk fibroin (SFGMA), oxidized carboxymethyl cellulose (OCMC), and aldehyde-functionalized glycyrrhizic acid (AGA). SFGMA formed a photocrosslinked covalent network, whereas OCMC and AGA introduced dynamic Schiff-base interactions with silk fibroin amines. Notably, AGA served as a structural-bioactive component by participating in network formation while retaining glycyrrhizic-acid-associated antioxidant and immunomodulatory activity. The SCG precursor exhibited syringe deliverability and rapid in situ photocuring, while the resulting hydrogel showed dynamic self-recovery, enhanced mechanical stability, interconnected porosity, controlled swelling and degradation, and good cyto- and hemocompatibility. In vitro, AGA-containing hydrogels markedly reduced reactive oxygen species and favored an M2-like macrophage phenotype, characterized by increased CD206, Arg-1, IL-10, and TGF-β expression together with reduced CD86, iNOS, and IL-6 expression. In LPS-stimulated macrophages, AGA-containing formulations also decreased extracellular HMGB1 levels. SCG further enhanced endothelial migration and tube formation. In a streptozotocin-induced diabetic full-thickness wound model, SCG promoted early wound-area reduction and significantly improved histological re-epithelialization, collagen matrix deposition, and vascularization, accompanied by reduced inflammatory-marker expression and increased M2-associated tissue markers. Collectively, these findings identify AGA as a promising structural-pharmacological building block for multifunctional hydrogel design and support SCG as an in situ-forming wound dressing for diabetic wound repair.

#### 摘要

Biodegradable Zn has attracted growing attention as a bone implant material because of its appropriate degradation rate and favourable mechanical properties. In this study, we used plasma electrolytic oxidation (PEO) followed by sulfurization to create a ZnO/ZnS heterostructured coating on Zn (S-PEO-Zn) for orthopedic applications. Surface characterization showed that the coating markedly increased surface roughness and improved wettability. This optimized interface effectively mitigated Zn-induced cytotoxicity and established a favourable local ionic microenvironment. Through controlled Zn ion release, S-PEO-Zn promoted macrophage polarization toward an anti-inflammatory phenotype, associated with downregulation of NF-κB-related inflammatory signalling. Furthermore, S-PEO-Zn enhanced endothelial cell migration and tube formation and upregulated angiogenesis-related gene expression in HUVECs. Meanwhile, S-PEO-Zn enhanced alkaline phosphatase activity and the expression of osteogenesis-related genes in C3H10T1/2 cells. Mouse air-pouch and rat femoral implantation models further demonstrated the favourable immunomodulatory and bone-regenerative effects of S-PEO-Zn in vivo, respectively. Overall, this work demonstrates that ZnO/ZnS-coated Zn represents a promising strategy for orthopedic applications by restoring the immune-vascular-bone coupling cascade.

#### [A] Management of vascular malformations and hemangiomas of the head and neck--an update.

- PMID: 23486379
- Domain: Brain AVM / AVM
- Domain 依据: Arteriovenous malformation expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 静脉畸形基础与治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Current opinion in otolaryngology & head and neck surgery
- 作者: Eivazi B, Werner JA
- 发表日期: 2013 Apr
- DOI: 10.1097/MOO.0b013e32835e15a9
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23486379/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-10

#### 摘要

PURPOSE OF REVIEW: We are witnesses of improved therapeutic strategies and intensified multidisciplinary and international exchange in the field of vascular anomalies of the head and neck region. This review summarizes today's 'state of the art' in treatment and points out recent developments in clinical management of hemangiomas and vascular malformations of the head and neck.
RECENT FINDINGS: Despite the general acceptance of the 'Hamburg classification', inappropriate use of the term 'hemangioma' is still found in scientific correspondences. The majority of problematic infantile hemangiomas are nowadays successfully treated with propranolol. Topical use of β blockers is the most recent development for hemangiomas. The understanding of the condition called 'localized intravascular coagulopathy' has significantly improved the clinical management of venous malformations apart from the introduction of sophisticated ethanol-based embolization agents. No significant improvement in the management of lymphatic or capillary malformations of the head and neck has been observed within recent years. The liquid embolization agent Onyx has become popular in the treatment of extracranial arteriovenous malformations (AVM), but can be associated with severe perivascular inflammation.
SUMMARY: β Blockers are the first-line modality for infantile hemangiomas, which require active treatment. Reports on pharmacotherapy of lymphatic malformations with sildenafil are subject to critical evaluation regarding drug safety. Pulsed dye laser treatment is still the 'gold standard' for capillary malformations. Surgical excision combined with intravascular embolization is treatment of choice for AVM with the knowledge that extensive AVM are still not curable.

#### [A] Baicalein-loaded procyanidins nanoparticles alleviate neuroinflammation in Parkinson's disease by restoring microglial 8-oxoguanine DNA glycosylase 1.

- PMID: 42815649
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate

- 作者: Zhou H, Song Y, Gao Y, Yao J, Liu G, Tang X, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 10
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jconrel.2026.115420
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42815649/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-10

#### 摘要

Microglial NLRP3 inflammasome activation and M1 polarization, driven by unresolved 8-oxoguanine (8-oxoG) DNA damage resulting from downregulated 8-oxoguanine DNA glycosylase 1 (OGG1), are key drivers of neuroinflammation in Parkinson's disease (PD). Baicalein (BAI) can restore OGG1 and interrupt this pathological cycle, but its poor solubility and low brain accumulation limit its therapeutic efficacy. Procyanidins (PC), a class of flavanol polyphenols with neuroprotective and anti-inflammatory effects, can self-assemble into nanoparticles (PC NPs) to encapsulate hydrophobic BAI while exerting synergistic therapeutic effects. However, PC NPs alone fail to overcome three sequential biological barriers for PD treatment: systemic clearance, blood brain barrier (BBB) transcytosis, and selective microglial uptake. To tackle these challenges, we engineered a cascade-delivery nanoplatform named RVG/MG1-M@PC@BAI NPs for the co-delivery of BAI and PC. In this system, PC forms self-assembled inner core to encapsulate BAI. This core is further coated with an immune-evasive hybrid membrane (M) surface-functionalized with RVG29 peptide for BBB penetration and MG1 peptide for selective M1 microglial uptake. In vitro and in vivo characterization demonstrated that the nanoplatform effectively penetrates the BBB and is preferentially internalized by microglia. Pharmacological evaluation demonstrated that the nanoplatform efficiently upregulates OGG1 expression, thereby reducing 8-oxoG accumulation, suppressing aberrant NLRP3 inflammasome activation, restoring the physiological balance of microglial M1/M2 phenotypes, and ultimately alleviating neuroinflammation. Safety assessment confirmed its favorable biocompatibility. In conclusion, RVG/MG1-M@PC@BAI NPs represent an OGG1-targeted synergistic nanoplatform, providing a promising strategy for PD therapy.

#### [A] Disulfidptosis-Driven Immune-Activated Multimodal Therapy for Glioblastoma with Systemic Safety Control.

- DOI: 10.1021/acsnano.5c20124
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43007758/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-11

#### 摘要

Effective glioblastoma therapy requires simultaneous solutions to poor blood-brain barrier (BBB) penetration, insufficient tumor-selective killing, and treatment-related systemic toxicity. We hypothesized that a BBB-penetrating nanoplatform capable of generating distinct H2S exposures in tumor and normal tissues could enhance antitumor efficacy while mitigating doxorubicin (DOX)-induced cardiotoxicity. Here, DOX-loaded metastable molybdenum disulfide nanoflowers functionalized with an I6P8 peptide (DOX@MSI) were developed. After BBB transport and tumor accumulation, the acidic, H2O2-rich, and GSH-rich microenvironment accelerates nanoplatform degradation, promoting GSH depletion and enhanced H2S/DOX release. High local H2S upregulates xCT, while H2S/DOX-mediated mitochondrial inhibition suppresses NADPH regeneration, synergistically triggering disulfidptosis. Meanwhile, H2S suppresses the JAK2/STAT3/IDO axis, alleviating DOX-induced immunosuppression and enhancing immunogenic cell death and T-cell-mediated antitumor immunity. In contrast, slower degradation under physiological conditions enables sustained low-level H2S release, reducing the level of oxidative stress and ERK-associated apoptosis in cardiomyocytes. Together with NIR-enhanced photothermal therapy, DOX@MSI suppresses orthotopic glioblastoma while reducing cardiac injury, thereby integrating tumor-selective multimodal therapy with active organ protection.

#### [A] Beyond Prostate-Specific Membrane Antigen: The Design and Future of Novel Radiopharmaceuticals Targeting the Tumor Microenvironment.

- PMID: 43004914
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism

- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Cancer biotherapy & radiopharmaceuticals
- 作者: Fu F, Wang B, Ding X, Li K, Guo M, Guo C, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 10
- DOI: 10.1177/10849785261490722
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43004914/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-11

#### 摘要

Targeted radiopharmaceutical therapy has entered mainstream oncology through the clinical success of prostate-specific membrane antigen (PSMA)-directed imaging and [177Lu] Lu-PSMA-617 therapy. However, heterogeneous antigen expression, acquired resistance, lineage plasticity, and nonresponding lesions show that a cancer cell-centric strategy cannot fully address the ecological complexity of advanced malignancy. The tumor microenvironment (TME) offers a complementary target space because stromal, vascular, immune, and chemokine-driven niches are functionally required for tumor growth and are often more genomically stable than malignant cells. This review synthesizes the biological rationale, radiochemical design, clinical translation, and current limitations of TME-targeted radiopharmaceuticals, focusing on fibroblast activation protein (FAP), integrins, and the CXCR4/CXCL12 axis. The authors discuss first-generation FAP inhibitor (FAPI) tracers and therapeutic limitations caused by short tumor residence, and then compare next-generation strategies, including dimerization, multimerization, albumin-binding motifs, peptide-based ligands such as FAP-2286, and newer FAPI-derived therapeutic constructs. They also reassess RGD-based integrin targeting as a clinically informative but heterogeneous angiogenesis platform, and summarize CXCR4-directed pentixather therapy in hematological malignancies and selected solid tumor models. Finally, they examine radionuclide selection, dosimetry, patient selection, and combination strategies with chemotherapy, antiangiogenic therapy, immune checkpoint blockade, cytokine therapy, and cell-based therapy. TME-targeted radiopharmaceuticals are unlikely to replace tumor cell-directed theranostics; rather, their most important role may be to reshape resistant tumor ecosystems and rationally extend precision radionuclide therapy beyond single-antigen paradigms.

#### [A] Targeting Systemic Immune Dysregulation in Cardiovascular Disease: From Mechanisms to Precision Therapeutic Strategies.

- PMID: 43009736
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗

- 作者: Li H, Ou Y, Zhu X, Fan G, Li L
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 11
- DOI: 10.1007/s10557-026-07985-1
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43009736/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-12

#### 摘要

Cardiovascular diseases remain a leading cause of morbidity and mortality despite advances in conventional pharmacological therapy. Persistent inflammation and immune dysregulation contribute to residual cardiovascular risk, disease heterogeneity, and variable therapeutic responses. Increasing evidence links cardiovascular injury to systemic immune and immunometabolic disturbances arising from the kidney, liver, brain, hematopoietic and lymphoid organs, gut, and adipose tissue. This review summarizes how systemic immune disturbances contribute to cardiovascular remodeling through altered immune-cell activation, impaired inflammatory resolution, metabolic stress, and pathological immune memory. We discuss the contributions of kidney-, liver-, neuroimmune-, gut-adipose-, and hematopoietic-associated abnormalities to major cardiovascular phenotypes, including heart failure with preserved ejection fraction, atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease, cardiorenal syndrome, metabolic cardiomyopathy, and aging-associated cardiovascular disease. These systemic abnormalities converge on common cardiovascular effector mechanisms, including endothelial dysfunction, inflammatory-cell recruitment, macrophage activation, fibroblast remodeling, extracellular matrix deposition, and cardiomyocyte dysfunction. We further examine emerging pharmacological strategies targeting systemic immune dysregulation, including cytokine inhibition, inflammasome modulation, metabolic interventions, and therapies targeting maladaptive immune memory. Although several approaches have demonstrated clinical benefit, effective translation requires identification of biologically defined patient subgroups, reliable biomarkers, and appropriate assessment of therapeutic response and safety. Integrating immune profiling with cardiovascular pharmacotherapy may facilitate a transition from nonspecific anti-inflammatory approaches toward precision modulation of pathological immune networks.
