#### 摘要

Post-stroke cognitive impairment (PSCI) is a common and highly disabling complication of stroke. Current research on its molecular mechanisms has largely focused on neuroinflammatory cascades and blood-brain barrier disruption caused by microglial polarization, while the role of cellular metabolism remains underexplored. Metabolic reprogramming is a coordinated network-level adjustment of carbohydrate, lipid, and amino acid metabolism, and has recently become a research hotspot in neurological diseases, particularly centered on phenotypic switching of microglia following metabolic reprogramming. As the center of cellular energy metabolism, mitochondria are closely linked to normal cellular bioenergetics through biogenesis, motility, and quality control; disruption of any of these links after brain injury is intimately associated with neuronal activity. In this review, we adopt a metabolism-oriented perspective to explore the changes in the brain microenvironment after metabolic reprogramming, connect the critical role of mitochondrial dysfunction in disease development, and deeply analyze the core positions of both in the pathogenesis of PSCI, presenting the complex regulatory network linking metabolic disturbances and mitochondrial dysfunction, as described in the Graphical Abstract. Furthermore, we discuss the future prospects of emerging technologies such as mitochondrial transplantation and stem cell therapy, aiming to translate theoretical advances into clinical applications for PSCI.

#### [A] Glycosaminoglycan-Induced Macrophage Reprogramming Orchestrates Pro-Regenerative Crosstalk with Fibroblasts.

- PMID: 43009078
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Acta biomaterialia
- 作者: Du Q, Mace K, Wong KFC, Lawler K, Kotowska A, AlRomaihi RA, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 10
- DOI: 10.1016/j.actbio.2026.11.011
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43009078/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-12

#### 摘要

Macrophages and fibroblasts play central roles in coordinating inflammation and tissue repair, whilst dysregulation of macrophage-fibroblast crosstalk drives pathological outcomes such as chronic inflammation and fibrosis. Glycosaminoglycans (GAGs), key components of the extracellular matrix (ECM), interact with cytokines and growth factors that modulate macrophage polarisation and direct fibroblast activity, making them attractive candidates for developing immune-instructive biomaterials. However, the optimal GAG compositions that promote macrophage-mediated repair and fibroblast-driven ECM remodelling remain unclear. Here, we report an unbiased screening of 82 surface-adsorbed GAG combinations to identify candidates capable of modulating macrophage-fibroblast interactions. Leveraging cytokine profiling, surface marker immunostaining, gene expression, principal component analysis (PCA), and hierarchical clustering, we identified 'hit' GAG combinations that induced distinct, non-canonical macrophage activation programmes characterised by coordinated inflammatory and regulatory signalling, alongside enhanced fibroblast proliferation. Shortlisted formulations were validated using expanded biological replication, orthogonal phenotypic, transcriptional and functional analyses, assessment in macrophage-fibroblast co-culture systems, and in an in vivo diabetic wound model. These data demonstrated that 'hit' GAG formulations modulated macrophage functional activation states, promoted collagen and ECM synthesis, facilitated vascular and lymphatic vessel formation, and accelerated wound closure. Complementary metabolomic profiling using OrbiSIMS and molecular formula prediction (MFP), revealed macrophage metabolic reprogramming as a potential mechanism underlying these pro-healing effects. Collectively, this study established an unbiased methodology for discovering immune-instructive GAGs in supporting pro-healing responses for potential wound-healing applications. STATEMENT OF SIGNIFICANCE: Glycosaminoglycans (GAGs) are key extracellular matrix components with significant potential to regulate immune responses. However, their immunomodulatory effects are highly context-dependent, varying with sulfation pattern, molecular composition, and the local microenvironment, making it challenging to identify GAG combinations that consistently promote pro-regenerative immune responses and tissue repair. Here, we developed an unbiased combinatorial screening platform to systematically identify GAG combinations that reprogram macrophages toward pro-regenerative functional states. The selected GAG combinations enhanced macrophage-fibroblast crosstalk in vitro and improved diabetic wound healing in vivo by promoting collagen deposition, angiogenesis, and lymphatic remodelling. OrbiSIMS metabolomics further revealed macrophage metabolic reprogramming associated with these regenerative responses. This work establishes a generalizable discovery framework for identifying immune-regulatory GAG formulations and provides new design principles for developing extracellular matrix-inspired immunomodulatory biomaterials for regenerative medicine.

#### [A] Hyperbaric oxygen therapy: can it prevent irradiation-induced necrosis?

- PMID: 22465460
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 静脉畸形基础与治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Experimental neurology
- 作者: Kuffler DP
- 发表日期: 2012 Jun
- DOI: 10.1016/j.expneurol.2012.03.011
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22465460/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Radiosurgery is an important non-invasive procedure for the treatment of tumors and vascular malformations. However, in addition to killing target tissues, cranial irradiation induces damage to adjacent healthy tissues leading to neurological deterioration in both pediatric and adult patients, which is poorly understood and insufficiently treatable. To minimize irradiation damage to healthy tissue, not the optimal therapeutic irradiation dose required to eliminate the target lesion is used but lower doses. Although the success rate of irradiation surgery is about 95%, 5% of patients suffer problems, most commonly neurological, that are thought to be a direct consequence of irradiation-induced inflammation. Although no direct correlation has been demonstrated, the appearance and disappearance of inflammation that develops following irradiation commonly parallel the appearance and disappearance of neurological side effects that are associated with the neurological function of the irradiated brain regions. These observations have led to the hypothesis that brain inflammation is causally related to the observed neurological side effects. Studies indicate that hyperbaric oxygen therapy (HBOT) applied after the appearance of irradiation-induced neurological side effects reduces the incidence and severity of those side effects. This may result from HBOT reducing inflammation, promoting angiogenesis, and influencing other cellular functions thereby suppressing events that cause the neurological side effects. However, it would be significantly better for the patient if rather than waiting for neurological side effects to become manifest they could be avoided. This review examines irradiation-induced neurological side effects, methods that minimize or resolve those side effects, and concludes with a discussion of whether HBOT applied following irradiation, but before manifestation of neurological side effects may prevent or reduce the appearance of irradiation-induced neurological side effects.

#### [A] Spontaneous complete regression of a cervical macrocystic lymphatic malformation following infection in an infant: A case report.

- PMID: 43022299
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 其他血管畸形/血管异常
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Radiology case reports
- 作者: Mohamed L, Kassimi M, Essamoud S, Berrada S, Zerhouni H
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec
- DOI: 10.1016/j.radcr.2026.09.061
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43022299/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Lymphatic malformations most frequently involve the head and neck region, accounting for nearly two-thirds of all reported cases, and are often diagnosed in infancy or early childhood. Approximately half of the lesions are present at birth, while up to 90% become clinically evident before the age of two years. Clinically, these lesions typically present as soft, painless, compressible masses that may progressively enlarge and occasionally cause functional complications such as airway compression, dysphagia, or cosmetic deformity. Although lymphatic malformations are histologically benign, their clinical course can be unpredictable. Episodes of infection, hemorrhage, or sudden enlargement may occur and can significantly modify the presentation of the lesion. Imaging modalities such as ultrasonography, computed tomography, and magnetic resonance imaging are commonly used to confirm the diagnosis and evaluate the extent of the lesion and its relationship with adjacent structures. Management strategies include surgical excision, sclerotherapy with various agents such as bleomycin or OK-432, or a combination of both approaches depending on lesion size and location. Nevertheless, spontaneous regression of lymphatic malformations has occasionally been described in the literature and appears to occur in a small proportion of patients. In some cases, inflammatory or infectious episodes may induce endothelial damage and fibrosis of the cystic spaces, leading to a reduction in lesion size or even complete resolution. Herein, we report the case of a large congenital cervical macrocystic lymphatic malformation in an infant that underwent complete regression following a superinfection treated with antibiotic therapy alone.

#### [A] PPARγ at the Nexus of Lipid Metabolism and Neuroinflammation in Alzheimer's Disease: A Longitudinal Multi-omics Analysis.

- PMID: 43024124
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Journal of molecular neuroscience : MN
- 作者: Huixuan H, Ruizhe W, Chunrong H, Na W, Tianwai D, Ludan Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 13
- DOI: 10.1007/s12031-026-02609-7
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43024124/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Peroxisome proliferator-activated receptor gamma (PPARγ) has emerged as an important regulator linking lipid metabolism and neuroimmune responses in Alzheimer's disease (AD). Increasing evidence suggests that disruption of the lipid-immune axis contributes to disease progression and may underlie the heterogeneous therapeutic responses observed with PPARγ-targeted interventions. Experimental studies indicate that PPARγ activation influences multiple biological processes relevant to AD, including lipid homeostasis, mitochondrial function, inflammatory signaling, and amyloid-β clearance. However, the translation of these findings into consistent clinical benefit remains limited by challenges such as poor blood-brain barrier penetration, systemic adverse effects, and disease stage-dependent therapeutic responses. Recent advances in multi-omics technologies and single-cell transcriptomics have provided new insights into the context-dependent roles of PPARγ in different neural cell populations, highlighting dynamic interactions between lipid metabolism, neuroinflammation, and cellular function throughout AD progression. These findings suggest that PPARγ-associated pathways may represent an important regulatory interface linking metabolic and inflammatory processes, while emphasizing the complexity of their temporal and cell-specific regulation. This review synthesizes current evidence from experimental studies, human multi-omics investigations, and clinical research to provide an updated perspective on the role of PPARγ in coordinating lipid metabolism and neuroinflammation in AD. In addition, we discuss the major translational challenges and emerging therapeutic strategies, including selective PPARγ modulation, improved central nervous system delivery, and precision treatment approaches tailored to disease stage.

#### [A] Stem cell therapy attenuates cerebellar histological alterations and blood-brain barrier disruption in experimental fibromyalgia: involvement of mast cells, autophagy, and NLRP3/IL-1β neuroinflammatory pathway.

- PMID: 43021681
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Tissue barriers
- 作者: Mokhemer SA, Abdelghany AK, Hassan SM, AbdelHaleem AH
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 13
- DOI: 10.1080/21688370.2026.2762719
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43021681/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Fibromyalgia is a prevalent and debilitating condition that results in generalized musculoskeletal pain, trouble sleeping, exhaustion, psychological challenges, and social problems. As a consequence of fibromyalgia's substantial therapeutic and socioeconomic burden caused by its high prevalence, uncertain etiology, wide spectrum of clinical manifestations, and sophisticated therapy, a considerable interest has emerged in investigating novel aspects of its pathophysiology, particularly those related to anxiety, depression, and cerebellar histological alterations. Such investigations may contribute to the development of effective therapeutic strategies, including the use of bone marrow-derived mesenchymal stem cells (BMSCs) as a promising treatment approach. Rats were randomly divided into three groups: control, fibromyalgia and fibromyalgia-stem cell groups. Behavioral assessment was carried out. Cerebellar cortices were collected for histological, immunohistochemical, and morphometric analysis. Fibromyalgia group revealed anxiety-like behavioral changes, and neurodegenerative changes with blood-brain barrier (BBB) affection. Furthermore, NLRP3 and IL-1β immune-expression were significantly increased, with significant increase in the total number of mast cells with marked degranulation. Additionally, there was a significant decrease in Calbindin D-28k (CB), Beclin-1 and LC3 immune-expression. Fibromyalgia-stem cells group revealed amelioration in behavioral alterations, histological improvement, significant decrease in CD117, NLRP3 and IL-1β immune-expression, besides a significant increase in CB, Beclin-1 and LC3 immune-expression. This study represents one of the first experimental studies investigating the potential therapeutic value of BMSCs therapy in fibromyalgia-related cerebellar damage through neuroprotective mechanisms involving enhancement of autophagy, mast cell deactivation, and suppression of NLRP3/IL-1β mediated neuroinflammation with improvement of BBB structural integrity.

#### [A] Microglial Metabolic Reprogramming Regulated by HIF-1α/HK2 Positive Feedback Inhibits Adult Hippocampal Neurogenesis in Diabetes-Associated Cognitive Dysfunction.

- PMID: 43020960
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Molecular neurobiology
- 作者: Jiang S, Zhao Y, Zhang Q, Zhang J, Gao Z, Luo Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 13
- DOI: 10.1007/s12035-026-06348-z
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43020960/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Diabetes-associated cognitive dysfunction (DACD) is a significant complication with few effective treatments and unclear underlying mechanism. The aim of this study was to investigate how hyperglycemia disrupts adult hippocampal neurogenesis (AHN) through metabolic-immune interactions, seeking to identify potential treatment targets for DACD. DACD models were developed using db/db mice or streptozotocin-induced mice, and their cognitive function was assessed through behavioral tests. Next, we integrated multi-omics analyses (transcriptomic, single-cell, and lactylome profiling) with metabolic studies in BV2 cells exposed to diabetic conditions. Pharmacological modulators (3-BP, C646, TSA) were used to clarify the underlying mechanisms. The mice with DACD demonstrated cognitive deficits, impaired AHN, and activation of hippocampal microglia. Transcriptomic analysis revealed glycolysis as the primary dysregulated pathway. Mechanistically, hyperglycemia/AGEs induced glycolytic reprogramming and increased lactate levels, which promoted HIF-1α lactylation at lysine residues-a modification catalyzed by p300 and opposed by histone deacetylases. This posttranslational modification was associated with HIF-1α stabilisation and with enhanced transcriptional occupancy at the HK2 promoter. This established a self-amplifying feedback loop, perpetuating microglial activation and disrupting the blood-brain barrier via CXCL10 secretion, which led to CD8+ T-cell infiltration. Systemic administration of the glycolytic inhibitor 3-BP disrupted the pathogenic circuit, thereby normalizing metabolism, attenuating neuroinflammation, and restoring cognitive function. HIF-1α lactylation is associated with HIF-1α stabilisation, acting as a potential metabolic-epigenetic mechanism that links glycolytic flux to sustained microglial activation, neuroinflammation and impaired AHN in DACD. Pharmacological inhibition of this axis attenuated neuroinflammation and improved cognitive outcomes in our model, suggesting a candidate therapeutic strategy for DACD.

#### [A] EXPRESS: Oligodendrocyte precursor cells in ischemic stroke: from biological properties and glial interactions to therapeutic implications.

- PMID: 43021509
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Journal of cerebral blood flow and metabolism : official journal of the International Society of Cerebral Blood Flow and Metabolism
- 作者: Wang X, Ding Z, Song Y, Wu Z, Yu W, Zhao P, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 12
- DOI: 10.1177/0271678X261508669
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43021509/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Ischemic stroke remains a leading cause of mortality and long-term disability worldwide. Although revascularization therapies have improved outcomes in certain patients, effective interventions for neurological deficits are still lacking. White matter injury plays a central role in driving persistent functional impairments. As the cellular foundation for myelin regeneration, oligodendrocyte precursor cells (OPCs) have emerged as a promising therapeutic target. This review systematically examines the pathophysiological alterations of OPCs following ischemic stroke, including their proliferation, migration, differentiation, and maturation into myelinating oligodendrocytes, and explores their dynamic interactions with the surrounding microenvironment, including microglia, astrocytes, neurons, and endothelial cells. Accumulating evidence indicates that OPCs not only facilitate myelin repair through differentiation into oligodendrocytes but also contribute to neuroprotection and vascular remodeling by secreting neurotrophic and pro-angiogenic factors. Therapeutic strategies aimed at preserving OPCs and enhancing their differentiation, including pharmacological therapy, cell transplantation, gene therapy, bioengineering and biomaterials technology, as well as rehabilitation and physical therapy, hold considerable promise for improving neurological recovery. By positioning OPC dysfunction as a driver of persistent disability, this review outlines a translational pathway toward therapies that restore white matter integrity and functional recovery after ischemic stroke.

#### [A] The blood-brain barrier imports diverse neuroprotective proteins through pathways impaired early in Alzheimer's disease models.

- PMID: 43014307
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism

- 主题: 跨领域顶刊启发
- 来源类型: 顶刊扩展
- 来源说明: Cell/Nature/Science生命科学顶刊
- 期刊: Cell
- 作者: Zhou H, Maynard JC, Pennacchio AJ, Apolonio A, Liu X, Yao L, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 11
- DOI: 10.1016/j.cell.2026.10.039
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43014307/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

The blood-brain barrier (BBB) has been defined primarily by what it excludes, yet the scale and function of physiological blood-to-brain protein transport remain largely unknown. Using bioorthogonal proteomics, we find that the healthy BBB samples 191 plasma proteins and permits 92 into the brain parenchyma. Enriched for carrier and metabolic functions, these proteins home to distinct brain cell types, such as apolipoprotein A1 (APOA1) to astrocytes and alpha-1 antitrypsin (SERPINA1) to microglia. This cell-type tropism relies on a BBB-parenchyma receptor hand-off: APOA1 crosses the BBB via SR-B1 and engages astrocytic ATP-binding cassette transporter A1 (ABCA1), a key Alzheimer's risk gene, driving cholesterol efflux that supports synapse integrity. Unlike LPS-induced nonspecific leakage, early-stage 5xFAD mice show selective BBB transport remodeling, with reduced APOA1 and increased SERPINA1 uptake. Hepatic APOA1 overexpression restores APOA1 in the 5xFAD brain, rescuing astrocytic, synaptic, and behavioral deficits. These findings recast the BBB as a selective interface that recruits neuroprotective circulatory proteins for brain health.

#### [A] Argatroban mitigates Klebsiella pneumoniae liver injury by attenuating ZBP1-dependent PANoptosis.

- PMID: 42753327
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: International immunopharmacology
- 作者: Wang H, Chen L, Wang H, Chang Z
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 15
- DOI: 10.1016/j.intimp.2026.117429
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42753327/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-15

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Klebsiella pneumoniae (K. pneumoniae) liver abscess (KPLA) is frequently accompanied by thrombophlebitis and systemic inflammation. Argatroban, a direct thrombin inhibitor, has anti-inflammatory potential, but its efficacy and mechanism in KPLA remain unclear.
METHODS: A murine KPLA model was established using a hypervirulent, patient-derived K. pneumoniae strain, with mice receiving vehicle or argatroban (10 mg kg-1/day, i.p.). Survival, liver pathology, vascular permeability, inflammatory responses, and transcriptomic changes were evaluated. Hepatocyte responses were investigated using KP-LPS-stimulated THLE-2 cells. ZBP1 localization, protein interactions, and functional dependence were assessed by immunofluorescence, co-immunoprecipitation, and ZBP1 silencing.
RESULTS: Argatroban significantly improved 72-h survival (P = 0.02), reduced hepatic necrosis, attenuated sinusoidal vascular leakage, and suppressed systemic inflammatory cytokine production in KPLA mice. RNA-seq revealed activation of innate immune-sensing pathways during infection, which were markedly attenuated by argatroban. ZBP1 and PANoptosis-associated molecules were increased in KPLA livers and reduced by argatroban. ZBP1 was predominantly localized in hepatocytes. In KP-LPS-stimulated THLE-2 cells, RIPK3, ASC, and Caspase-8 showed enhanced intracellular co-localization, while co-immunoprecipitation demonstrated ZBP1 interactions with RIPK3, ASC, and Caspase-8. ZBP1 silencing markedly reduced KP-LPS-induced activation of p-MLKL, cleaved GSDMD, and cleaved caspase-3, supporting ZBP1-dependent PANoptosis as a key driver of hepatocyte inflammatory injury. Mechanistically, argatroban inhibited TLR4 endocytosis and downstream TBK1/IRF3-STAT1 signaling, thereby suppressing ZBP1 induction.
CONCLUSIONS: Argatroban protects against KPLA-associated liver injury by inhibiting a TLR4 endocytosis-TBK1/IRF3-STAT1-ZBP1 axis and suppressing ZBP1-dependent PANoptosis, identifying PANoptosis as a potential therapeutic target in severe K. pneumoniae infection.

#### [A] Running title: The plasticity of CAFs in cancer development.

- PMID: 42998866
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Clinica chimica acta; international journal of clinical chemistry
- 作者: Bakhshandeh B, Jahanafrooz Z, Ghajari G, Mohtadi D, Rahmani-Kukia N, Abbasi A, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 07
- DOI: 10.1016/j.cca.2026.122960
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42998866/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Cancer is among the leading causes of death worldwide. The tumor microenvironment is a dynamic, multifaceted milieu where the cancer cells interact with diverse components. Resident stromal cell-derived cancer-associated fibroblasts are central to the crosstalk. There is variability in the origin, phenotype, and function of CAFs, which is influenced by signaling cues from cancer cells and the extracellular matrix. The presence of CAFs in the TME coincides with tumorigenesis, invasion, metastasis, and drug resistance. Many mechanisms are involved, such as metabolic reprogramming of glucose, amino acid, and lipid metabolism. For example, enhanced lactate production can contribute to a glucose-lactate shuttle that supports tumor growth. Dysregulation of normal ECM remodeling leads to a breakdown of the basement membrane, thereby enabling tumor cells to invade. Besides contributing to poor prognosis, CAFs may induce cancer cells to create vascular-like networks. They also recruit immunosuppressive cells and inhibit the immune response. Consequently, deciphering how the different factors affecting the fate and function of CAFs signal and modulate their activities is imperative in fully grasping the complex biology of cancer to seek more effective therapeutic options. By targeting key pathways, surface markers, and conversion of CAFs to a quiescent state, the therapeutic outcome of the treatments for cancers may be improved. In this review, we summarize current knowledge on the biology of cancer-associated fibroblasts (CAFs), highlighting how distinct CAF subtypes defined by markers such as α-SMA, FAP, and MHC II (apCAFs), as well as inflammatory (iCAFs) and myofibroblastic (myCAFs) profiles-modulate extracellular matrix remodeling, metabolism, angiogenesis, immune responses, and metastatic potential. Our detailed review goes beyond explaining the potential of CAFs in developing and progressing cancer and addresses areas of research that could enable the widespread translation of this science for effective cancer therapy. Translational work and well-designed clinical trials will be required to fully transform CAFs from facilitators of resistance to actionable targets.

#### [A] Disulfidptosis-Driven Immune-Activated Multimodal Therapy for Glioblastoma with Systemic Safety Control.

- PMID: 43007758
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: ACS nano
- 作者: Zhang M, Lin C, Ganbat K, Meng W, Kang H, Gao W, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 10
- DOI: 10.1021/acsnano.5c20124
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43007758/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-11

#### 摘要

Effective glioblastoma therapy requires simultaneous solutions to poor blood-brain barrier (BBB) penetration, insufficient tumor-selective killing, and treatment-related systemic toxicity. We hypothesized that a BBB-penetrating nanoplatform capable of generating distinct H2S exposures in tumor and normal tissues could enhance antitumor efficacy while mitigating doxorubicin (DOX)-induced cardiotoxicity. Here, DOX-loaded metastable molybdenum disulfide nanoflowers functionalized with an I6P8 peptide (DOX@MSI) were developed. After BBB transport and tumor accumulation, the acidic, H2O2-rich, and GSH-rich microenvironment accelerates nanoplatform degradation, promoting GSH depletion and enhanced H2S/DOX release. High local H2S upregulates xCT, while H2S/DOX-mediated mitochondrial inhibition suppresses NADPH regeneration, synergistically triggering disulfidptosis. Meanwhile, H2S suppresses the JAK2/STAT3/IDO axis, alleviating DOX-induced immunosuppression and enhancing immunogenic cell death and T-cell-mediated antitumor immunity. In contrast, slower degradation under physiological conditions enables sustained low-level H2S release, reducing the level of oxidative stress and ERK-associated apoptosis in cardiomyocytes. Together with NIR-enhanced photothermal therapy, DOX@MSI suppresses orthotopic glioblastoma while reducing cardiac injury, thereby integrating tumor-selective multimodal therapy with active organ protection.

#### 摘要

Post-stroke cognitive impairment (PSCI) is a common and highly disabling complication of stroke. Current research on its molecular mechanisms has largely focused on neuroinflammatory cascades and blood-brain barrier disruption caused by microglial polarization, while the role of cellular metabolism remains underexplored. Metabolic reprogramming is a coordinated network-level adjustment of carbohydrate, lipid, and amino acid metabolism, and has recently become a research hotspot in neurological diseases, particularly centered on phenotypic switching of microglia following metabolic reprogramming. As the center of cellular energy metabolism, mitochondria are closely linked to normal cellular bioenergetics through biogenesis, motility, and quality control; disruption of any of these links after brain injury is intimately associated with neuronal activity. In this review, we adopt a metabolism-oriented perspective to explore the changes in the brain microenvironment after metabolic reprogramming, connect the critical role of mitochondrial dysfunction in disease development, and deeply analyze the core positions of both in the pathogenesis of PSCI, presenting the complex regulatory network linking metabolic disturbances and mitochondrial dysfunction, as described in the Graphical Abstract. Furthermore, we discuss the future prospects of emerging technologies such as mitochondrial transplantation and stem cell therapy, aiming to translate theoretical advances into clinical applications for PSCI.

#### [A] Glycosaminoglycan-Induced Macrophage Reprogramming Orchestrates Pro-Regenerative Crosstalk with Fibroblasts.

- PMID: 43009078
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Acta biomaterialia
- 作者: Du Q, Mace K, Wong KFC, Lawler K, Kotowska A, AlRomaihi RA, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 10
- DOI: 10.1016/j.actbio.2026.11.011
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43009078/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-12

#### 摘要

Macrophages and fibroblasts play central roles in coordinating inflammation and tissue repair, whilst dysregulation of macrophage-fibroblast crosstalk drives pathological outcomes such as chronic inflammation and fibrosis. Glycosaminoglycans (GAGs), key components of the extracellular matrix (ECM), interact with cytokines and growth factors that modulate macrophage polarisation and direct fibroblast activity, making them attractive candidates for developing immune-instructive biomaterials. However, the optimal GAG compositions that promote macrophage-mediated repair and fibroblast-driven ECM remodelling remain unclear. Here, we report an unbiased screening of 82 surface-adsorbed GAG combinations to identify candidates capable of modulating macrophage-fibroblast interactions. Leveraging cytokine profiling, surface marker immunostaining, gene expression, principal component analysis (PCA), and hierarchical clustering, we identified 'hit' GAG combinations that induced distinct, non-canonical macrophage activation programmes characterised by coordinated inflammatory and regulatory signalling, alongside enhanced fibroblast proliferation. Shortlisted formulations were validated using expanded biological replication, orthogonal phenotypic, transcriptional and functional analyses, assessment in macrophage-fibroblast co-culture systems, and in an in vivo diabetic wound model. These data demonstrated that 'hit' GAG formulations modulated macrophage functional activation states, promoted collagen and ECM synthesis, facilitated vascular and lymphatic vessel formation, and accelerated wound closure. Complementary metabolomic profiling using OrbiSIMS and molecular formula prediction (MFP), revealed macrophage metabolic reprogramming as a potential mechanism underlying these pro-healing effects. Collectively, this study established an unbiased methodology for discovering immune-instructive GAGs in supporting pro-healing responses for potential wound-healing applications. STATEMENT OF SIGNIFICANCE: Glycosaminoglycans (GAGs) are key extracellular matrix components with significant potential to regulate immune responses. However, their immunomodulatory effects are highly context-dependent, varying with sulfation pattern, molecular composition, and the local microenvironment, making it challenging to identify GAG combinations that consistently promote pro-regenerative immune responses and tissue repair. Here, we developed an unbiased combinatorial screening platform to systematically identify GAG combinations that reprogram macrophages toward pro-regenerative functional states. The selected GAG combinations enhanced macrophage-fibroblast crosstalk in vitro and improved diabetic wound healing in vivo by promoting collagen deposition, angiogenesis, and lymphatic remodelling. OrbiSIMS metabolomics further revealed macrophage metabolic reprogramming associated with these regenerative responses. This work establishes a generalizable discovery framework for identifying immune-regulatory GAG formulations and provides new design principles for developing extracellular matrix-inspired immunomodulatory biomaterials for regenerative medicine.

#### [A] PPARγ at the Nexus of Lipid Metabolism and Neuroinflammation in Alzheimer's Disease: A Longitudinal Multi-omics Analysis.

- PMID: 43024124
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Journal of molecular neuroscience : MN
- 作者: Huixuan H, Ruizhe W, Chunrong H, Na W, Tianwai D, Ludan Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 13
- DOI: 10.1007/s12031-026-02609-7
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43024124/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Peroxisome proliferator-activated receptor gamma (PPARγ) has emerged as an important regulator linking lipid metabolism and neuroimmune responses in Alzheimer's disease (AD). Increasing evidence suggests that disruption of the lipid-immune axis contributes to disease progression and may underlie the heterogeneous therapeutic responses observed with PPARγ-targeted interventions. Experimental studies indicate that PPARγ activation influences multiple biological processes relevant to AD, including lipid homeostasis, mitochondrial function, inflammatory signaling, and amyloid-β clearance. However, the translation of these findings into consistent clinical benefit remains limited by challenges such as poor blood-brain barrier penetration, systemic adverse effects, and disease stage-dependent therapeutic responses. Recent advances in multi-omics technologies and single-cell transcriptomics have provided new insights into the context-dependent roles of PPARγ in different neural cell populations, highlighting dynamic interactions between lipid metabolism, neuroinflammation, and cellular function throughout AD progression. These findings suggest that PPARγ-associated pathways may represent an important regulatory interface linking metabolic and inflammatory processes, while emphasizing the complexity of their temporal and cell-specific regulation. This review synthesizes current evidence from experimental studies, human multi-omics investigations, and clinical research to provide an updated perspective on the role of PPARγ in coordinating lipid metabolism and neuroinflammation in AD. In addition, we discuss the major translational challenges and emerging therapeutic strategies, including selective PPARγ modulation, improved central nervous system delivery, and precision treatment approaches tailored to disease stage.

#### [A] Microglial Metabolic Reprogramming Regulated by HIF-1α/HK2 Positive Feedback Inhibits Adult Hippocampal Neurogenesis in Diabetes-Associated Cognitive Dysfunction.

- PMID: 43020960
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Molecular neurobiology
- 作者: Jiang S, Zhao Y, Zhang Q, Zhang J, Gao Z, Luo Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 13
- DOI: 10.1007/s12035-026-06348-z
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43020960/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

Diabetes-associated cognitive dysfunction (DACD) is a significant complication with few effective treatments and unclear underlying mechanism. The aim of this study was to investigate how hyperglycemia disrupts adult hippocampal neurogenesis (AHN) through metabolic-immune interactions, seeking to identify potential treatment targets for DACD. DACD models were developed using db/db mice or streptozotocin-induced mice, and their cognitive function was assessed through behavioral tests. Next, we integrated multi-omics analyses (transcriptomic, single-cell, and lactylome profiling) with metabolic studies in BV2 cells exposed to diabetic conditions. Pharmacological modulators (3-BP, C646, TSA) were used to clarify the underlying mechanisms. The mice with DACD demonstrated cognitive deficits, impaired AHN, and activation of hippocampal microglia. Transcriptomic analysis revealed glycolysis as the primary dysregulated pathway. Mechanistically, hyperglycemia/AGEs induced glycolytic reprogramming and increased lactate levels, which promoted HIF-1α lactylation at lysine residues-a modification catalyzed by p300 and opposed by histone deacetylases. This posttranslational modification was associated with HIF-1α stabilisation and with enhanced transcriptional occupancy at the HK2 promoter. This established a self-amplifying feedback loop, perpetuating microglial activation and disrupting the blood-brain barrier via CXCL10 secretion, which led to CD8+ T-cell infiltration. Systemic administration of the glycolytic inhibitor 3-BP disrupted the pathogenic circuit, thereby normalizing metabolism, attenuating neuroinflammation, and restoring cognitive function. HIF-1α lactylation is associated with HIF-1α stabilisation, acting as a potential metabolic-epigenetic mechanism that links glycolytic flux to sustained microglial activation, neuroinflammation and impaired AHN in DACD. Pharmacological inhibition of this axis attenuated neuroinflammation and improved cognitive outcomes in our model, suggesting a candidate therapeutic strategy for DACD.

### mTOR feedback / inhibitor resistance（1）

#### [A] Running title: The plasticity of CAFs in cancer development.

- PMID: 42998866
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Clinica chimica acta; international journal of clinical chemistry
- 作者: Bakhshandeh B, Jahanafrooz Z, Ghajari G, Mohtadi D, Rahmani-Kukia N, Abbasi A, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 07
- DOI: 10.1016/j.cca.2026.122960
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42998866/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Cancer is among the leading causes of death worldwide. The tumor microenvironment is a dynamic, multifaceted milieu where the cancer cells interact with diverse components. Resident stromal cell-derived cancer-associated fibroblasts are central to the crosstalk. There is variability in the origin, phenotype, and function of CAFs, which is influenced by signaling cues from cancer cells and the extracellular matrix. The presence of CAFs in the TME coincides with tumorigenesis, invasion, metastasis, and drug resistance. Many mechanisms are involved, such as metabolic reprogramming of glucose, amino acid, and lipid metabolism. For example, enhanced lactate production can contribute to a glucose-lactate shuttle that supports tumor growth. Dysregulation of normal ECM remodeling leads to a breakdown of the basement membrane, thereby enabling tumor cells to invade. Besides contributing to poor prognosis, CAFs may induce cancer cells to create vascular-like networks. They also recruit immunosuppressive cells and inhibit the immune response. Consequently, deciphering how the different factors affecting the fate and function of CAFs signal and modulate their activities is imperative in fully grasping the complex biology of cancer to seek more effective therapeutic options. By targeting key pathways, surface markers, and conversion of CAFs to a quiescent state, the therapeutic outcome of the treatments for cancers may be improved. In this review, we summarize current knowledge on the biology of cancer-associated fibroblasts (CAFs), highlighting how distinct CAF subtypes defined by markers such as α-SMA, FAP, and MHC II (apCAFs), as well as inflammatory (iCAFs) and myofibroblastic (myCAFs) profiles-modulate extracellular matrix remodeling, metabolism, angiogenesis, immune responses, and metastatic potential. Our detailed review goes beyond explaining the potential of CAFs in developing and progressing cancer and addresses areas of research that could enable the widespread translation of this science for effective cancer therapy. Translational work and well-designed clinical trials will be required to fully transform CAFs from facilitators of resistance to actionable targets.

#### [A] Lower Extremity Overgrowth Associated with a Somatic MTOR Variant.

- PMID: 43011624

- 主题: 其他血管畸形/血管异常
- 来源类型: 近期补位

- 作者: Ad M, Givant M, Singhal D, Greene AK
- 发表日期: 2026 Sep
- DOI: 10.1097/jova.0000000000000152
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43011624/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-12

#### 摘要

There are many causes of lower extremity overgrowth. We present a 69-year-old female who was referred to the Boston Lymphatic Center due to left lower extremity enlargement. Her history and physical examination were inconsistent with lymphedema and a lymphoscintigram excluded this condition. She was hypothesized to have a condition on the PIK3CA-related overgrowth spectrum. The overgrown tissue was resected and to obtain a diagnosis, it was subjected to targeted next-generation sequencing, which showed an MTOR (NM_001386500:c.6644C>T (p.Ser2215Phe)) variant. This mutation has not been previously associated with lower extremity overgrowth.

#### [A] Truncal Marginal Veins in CLOVES and PIK3CA-Related Overgrowth Spectrum: Anatomic Patterns and Potential Clinical Insights for Diagnosis and Endovascular Treatments.

- PMID: 43020463
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 静脉畸形基础与治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Journal of vascular and interventional radiology : JVIR
- 作者: Al-Ani AT, Megahed A, Alomari AI, Chaudry G, Shaikh R, Amarneh M, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 12
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jvir.2026.109192
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43020463/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

PURPOSE: To describe the anatomy, clinical features, and endovascular treatment outcomes of truncal marginal veins (TMVs) involving the chest or abdominal wall in patients with Congenital Lipomatous Overgrowth, Vascular Malformations, Epidermal nevi, and Skeletal abnormalities (CLOVES) and PIK3CA-related overgrowth spectrum (PROS).
MATERIALS AND METHODS: The Vascular Anomalies Center database was retrospectively reviewed from 2008 to January 2026 to identify patients with TMVs. Imaging was reviewed for TMV diameter, origin, and insertion. Patient demographics, endovascular treatments, and follow-up data were analyzed.
RESULTS: Thirty-two patients (53% male; median age 4.2 years; CLOVES [n=26], PROS [n=6]) with 44 TMVs were included. TMVs were unilateral in 20 patients and bilateral in 12. The median TMV diameter was 9.0 mm. TMVs most commonly originated from the abdominal wall (n=21), followed by the chest wall (n=9), saphenofemoral junction (n=9), and lateral marginal vein (n=5). The most common insertion site was axillary vein (n=39) followed by the abdominal wall (n=5). Thirty embolization procedures were performed in 25 patients; a single embolic agent was used in 20 procedures and multiple agents in 10. The most commonly used agents were coils (n=26) and n-butyl cyanoacrylate glue (n=9). No treatment-related adverse events occurred. Among 26 treated TMVs with imaging follow-up (median 23 months), partial recanalization occurred in two TMVs (8%), whereas 24 (92%) remained completely occluded.
CONCLUSION: In this predominantly CLOVES cohort, TMVs most often arise from the abdominal wall and drain into the axillary vein, and endovascular treatment of TMVs appears safe, effective, and durable on imaging follow-up.

#### [A] The blood-brain barrier imports diverse neuroprotective proteins through pathways impaired early in Alzheimer's disease models.

- PMID: 43014307
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 跨领域顶刊启发
- 来源类型: 顶刊扩展
- 来源说明: Cell/Nature/Science生命科学顶刊
- 期刊: Cell
- 作者: Zhou H, Maynard JC, Pennacchio AJ, Apolonio A, Liu X, Yao L, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 11
- DOI: 10.1016/j.cell.2026.10.039
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43014307/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-14

#### 摘要

The blood-brain barrier (BBB) has been defined primarily by what it excludes, yet the scale and function of physiological blood-to-brain protein transport remain largely unknown. Using bioorthogonal proteomics, we find that the healthy BBB samples 191 plasma proteins and permits 92 into the brain parenchyma. Enriched for carrier and metabolic functions, these proteins home to distinct brain cell types, such as apolipoprotein A1 (APOA1) to astrocytes and alpha-1 antitrypsin (SERPINA1) to microglia. This cell-type tropism relies on a BBB-parenchyma receptor hand-off: APOA1 crosses the BBB via SR-B1 and engages astrocytic ATP-binding cassette transporter A1 (ABCA1), a key Alzheimer's risk gene, driving cholesterol efflux that supports synapse integrity. Unlike LPS-induced nonspecific leakage, early-stage 5xFAD mice show selective BBB transport remodeling, with reduced APOA1 and increased SERPINA1 uptake. Hepatic APOA1 overexpression restores APOA1 in the 5xFAD brain, rescuing astrocytic, synaptic, and behavioral deficits. These findings recast the BBB as a selective interface that recruits neuroprotective circulatory proteins for brain health.

#### [A] Argatroban mitigates Klebsiella pneumoniae liver injury by attenuating ZBP1-dependent PANoptosis.

- PMID: 42753327
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: International immunopharmacology
- 作者: Wang H, Chen L, Wang H, Chang Z
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 15
- DOI: 10.1016/j.intimp.2026.117429
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42753327/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-15

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Klebsiella pneumoniae (K. pneumoniae) liver abscess (KPLA) is frequently accompanied by thrombophlebitis and systemic inflammation. Argatroban, a direct thrombin inhibitor, has anti-inflammatory potential, but its efficacy and mechanism in KPLA remain unclear.
METHODS: A murine KPLA model was established using a hypervirulent, patient-derived K. pneumoniae strain, with mice receiving vehicle or argatroban (10 mg kg-1/day, i.p.). Survival, liver pathology, vascular permeability, inflammatory responses, and transcriptomic changes were evaluated. Hepatocyte responses were investigated using KP-LPS-stimulated THLE-2 cells. ZBP1 localization, protein interactions, and functional dependence were assessed by immunofluorescence, co-immunoprecipitation, and ZBP1 silencing.
RESULTS: Argatroban significantly improved 72-h survival (P = 0.02), reduced hepatic necrosis, attenuated sinusoidal vascular leakage, and suppressed systemic inflammatory cytokine production in KPLA mice. RNA-seq revealed activation of innate immune-sensing pathways during infection, which were markedly attenuated by argatroban. ZBP1 and PANoptosis-associated molecules were increased in KPLA livers and reduced by argatroban. ZBP1 was predominantly localized in hepatocytes. In KP-LPS-stimulated THLE-2 cells, RIPK3, ASC, and Caspase-8 showed enhanced intracellular co-localization, while co-immunoprecipitation demonstrated ZBP1 interactions with RIPK3, ASC, and Caspase-8. ZBP1 silencing markedly reduced KP-LPS-induced activation of p-MLKL, cleaved GSDMD, and cleaved caspase-3, supporting ZBP1-dependent PANoptosis as a key driver of hepatocyte inflammatory injury. Mechanistically, argatroban inhibited TLR4 endocytosis and downstream TBK1/IRF3-STAT1 signaling, thereby suppressing ZBP1 induction.
CONCLUSIONS: Argatroban protects against KPLA-associated liver injury by inhibiting a TLR4 endocytosis-TBK1/IRF3-STAT1-ZBP1 axis and suppressing ZBP1-dependent PANoptosis, identifying PANoptosis as a potential therapeutic target in severe K. pneumoniae infection.

- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: Methodist DeBakey cardiovascular journal
- 作者: Almutlaq M, Alhumaidan H, Maghrabi MK, Alenazy A
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.14797/mdcvj.1953
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42997530/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Immunoglobulin A (IgA) vasculitis (IgAV), formerly known as Henoch-Schönlein purpura, is a systemic small-vessel vasculitis caused by IgA immune-complex deposition; cardiac involvement is exceedingly rare and incompletely characterized. We describe a 41-year-old woman with biopsy-proven IgA nephropathy who presented with progressive dyspnea, hemoptysis, and a painful purpuric rash and was found to have severe multisystem IgAV with simultaneous cardiac, pulmonary, renal, and cutaneous involvement. High-sensitivity troponin T peaked at 20,208 ng/L and N-terminal pro-B-type natriuretic peptide at 49,428 pg/mL. Cardiac magnetic resonance imaging showed a left ventricular ejection fraction of 25%, diffuse mid-wall and epicardial late gadolinium enhancement with abnormal T1 and T2 values, and a 25 × 15 mm apical thrombus. Skin biopsy confirmed leukocytoclastic vasculitis and bronchoscopy confirmed diffuse alveolar hemorrhage, while multimodality imaging and a negative serologic work-up excluded cardiac sarcoidosis, antineutrophil cytoplasmic antibody (ANCA)-associated vasculitis, and anti-glomerular basement membrane disease. The patient was treated with pulse methylprednisolone, plasma exchange, rituximab, and cautious anticoagulation, with resolution of alveolar hemorrhage, a progressive decline in cardiac biomarkers, resolution of the left ventricular thrombus, and partial recovery of left ventricular systolic function. This case expands the recognized spectrum of cardiovascular involvement in adult IgAV and highlights the value of multimodality imaging and multidisciplinary management in establishing the diagnosis and guiding therapy.

### [A] The Battle Against Systemic Sclerosis: A Case Report of a Patient With Scleroderma Renal Crisis.

- PMID: 42999723

- 期刊: Cureus
- 作者: Elkurwi N, Garmeh Z, Elkurwi Q, Abu Jawhar ZH, Wajdlich M
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct
- DOI: 10.7759/cureus.117638
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42999723/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Systemic scleroderma (SSc) is an uncommon autoimmune disorder that causes excessive fibrosis of the skin and internal organs, especially the lungs, kidneys, and heart. Scleroderma renal crisis (SRC) is a rare and potentially fatal complication of SSc. In recent years, there has been an increasing interest in SSc treatment, but so far, the targeted management approach has been lacking. We report a 44-year-old male patient with overlap syndrome (systemic scleroderma, Sjogren's syndrome, and myositis), who underwent treatment with therapeutic plasma exchange (TPE) and received hemodialysis, angiotensin-converting enzyme inhibitors, glucocorticosteroids, and cyclophosphamide. The patient presented prominent cutaneous scleroderma with sclerodactyly and Raynaud's phenomenon. Despite the initial stabilization of the patient's condition on the multimodal pharmacotherapy combined with TPE, he later died from multi-organ failure. Although TPE performed during the early course of the disease has been reported as a potential adjunctive therapy, its efficacy and long-term benefits remain uncertain. Given the complexity of our patient's presentation, including scleroderma overlap syndrome and advanced, irreversible organ damage, it is difficult to determine whether TPE provided a meaningful clinical benefit.

### [A] Right-Sided Colonic Wall Thickening on Abdominal Computed Tomography as a Potential Adjunctive Imaging Clue in Thrombotic Microangiopathy Presenting With Gastrointestinal Symptoms.

- PMID: 42999465
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory

- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: Cureus
- 作者: Kitamura K, Hayashi K, Suzuki T
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct
- DOI: 10.7759/cureus.117615
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42999465/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Thrombotic microangiopathy (TMA) presenting with gastrointestinal symptoms poses a diagnostic challenge because Shiga toxin-producing Escherichia coli-associated hemolytic uremic syndrome (STEC-HUS) and acquired thrombotic thrombocytopenic purpura (aTTP) share overlapping clinical features but require distinct management. We report the case of a 22-year-old woman who presented with abdominal pain and hematochezia and subsequently developed TMA. Abdominal computed tomography (CT) demonstrated continuous edematous wall thickening from the terminal ileum and cecum to the ascending colon, raising early suspicion for STEC-HUS. However, a high PLASMIC score prompted urgent empirical plasma exchange, which was unfortunately complicated by anaphylactic shock. Subsequent ADAMTS13 testing revealed preserved activity, excluding severe ADAMTS13 deficiency and strongly arguing against acute aTTP. A clinical diagnosis of probable STEC-HUS was made. To further assess the diagnostic value of CT, we reviewed eight consecutive TMA cases with gastrointestinal symptoms at our institution. Right-sided colonic wall thickening was present in all five STEC-HUS cases but absent in all three aTTP cases, each of whom demonstrated severe ADAMTS13 deficiency. These preliminary findings suggest that right hemicolon-predominant bowel wall thickening may serve as an early adjunctive imaging clue supporting the diagnosis of STEC-HUS in patients with TMA and gastrointestinal symptoms, rather than a definitive discriminator between STEC-HUS and aTTP. This index case and institutional review highlight the potential utility of abdominal CT as an adjunctive imaging clue in guiding early diagnostic reasoning in patients with TMA and gastrointestinal manifestations. Validation in larger cohorts with microbiologically confirmed STEC-HUS and ADAMTS13-confirmed aTTP is required to establish the true diagnostic performance of this finding.

### [A] Shaping the role of plasma exchange in autoimmune neurology: lessons learned from 67 years and over 90,000 procedures.

- PMID: 41466846
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Independent plasma exchange/blood exchange research interest; not disease-domain relevance
- 优先级依据: A: plasma exchange/plasmapheresis/blood exchange/immunoadsorption
- 主题: 换血/血浆置换疗法
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: Therapeutic advances in neurological disorders
- 作者: Khatri BO, Berkovich R, Raval JS, Dukic M, Sershon L
- 发表日期: 2025
- DOI: 10.1177/17562864251401510
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41466846/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-09

#### 摘要

Therapeutic plasma exchange (PLEX) is a powerful and fast-acting immunomodulating therapy that is underutilized for autoimmune neurological disorders. Here, we present the largest collection of real-world experiences with PLEX procedures to date in the treatment of autoimmune neurological conditions, supporting its safety and clinical benefits with patient cases and corresponding patient videos. Our collective real-world experience with PLEX spans over 67 years, 90,210 procedures, and includes nine double-blind randomized controlled and unblinded studies serving as principal investigators. Case histories and videos of our patients demonstrate when and how PLEX should be used, identify barriers to using PLEX, and ways to overcome these barriers. Specific protocol details are shared of how to treat an acute or chronic phase of a disease. If used appropriately and early in the disease course for both acute and chronic progressive phases, PLEX can safely change the trajectory of many autoimmune neurological disorders in both outpatient and inpatient settings.

### [A] Pearls & Oy-sters: Anti-GABAA Receptor Encephalitis Presenting as Fulminant Hemicerebellitis in a 5-Year-Old Girl.

- PMID: 43002719
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 期刊: Neurology
- 作者: Krohn KE, Luke R, Lacy A, Santoro JD, Honeycutt J, Cope-Yokoyama S, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec 08
- DOI: 10.1212/WNL.0000000000218778
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43002719/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-10

#### 摘要

A previously healthy 5-year-old girl presented with several days of fever/malaise followed by acute neurologic decline. Head CT demonstrated fulminant right cerebellitis with edema and fourth-ventricle effacement concerning for impending herniation. She underwent emergent posterior fossa decompression. CSF showed mild pleocytosis, and MRI revealed right cerebellar nonenhancing T2/fluid-attenuated inversion recovery hyperintense lesions with evolving supratentorial cortical involvement. Despite empiric antimicrobials and initial immunotherapy (high-dose IV methylprednisolone and tocilizumab followed by IV immunoglobulin/plasma exchange), she developed progressive neuroinflammation with new seizures and markedly elevated CSF IL-6, prompting escalation to additional IL-6-directed therapy with tocilizumab and rescue intrathecal dexamethasone. She subsequently stabilized with radiographic improvement and meaningful neurologic recovery. Expanded antibody testing later confirmed anti-GABAA receptor (anti-GABAAR) encephalitis. This case highlights fulminant cerebellitis as a life-threatening presentation of anti-GABAAR encephalitis and underscores the importance of early posterior fossa decompression, parallel diagnostic evaluation (including expanded antibody panels), and coordinated, time-sensitive immunotherapy escalation in select critically ill presentations.

### [A] Monoclonal Gammopathy of Neurologic Significance: A Practical Framework for Diagnosis and Management.

- PMID: 43002727
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Independent plasma exchange/blood exchange research interest; not disease-domain relevance
- 优先级依据: A: plasma exchange/plasmapheresis/blood exchange/immunoadsorption
- 主题: 换血/血浆置换疗法
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: JCO oncology practice
- 作者: Bhutani M, Porebski M, Tedder B, Desai U, Paul B
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 09
- DOI: 10.1200/OP-26-00658
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43002727/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-10

#### 摘要

Monoclonal gammopathy of neurologic significance (MGNS) refers to peripheral neuropathies that are attributable to a monoclonal immunoglobulin produced by an underlying clonal plasma cell population or B-cell population in the absence of multiple myeloma, amyloid light chain amyloidosis, or polyneuropathy, organomegaly, endocrinopathy, monoclonal protein, skin changes syndrome. Because the prevalence of both monoclonal gammopathy of undetermined significance (MGUS) and peripheral neuropathy increases with age, their coexistence is frequently incidental. However, population-based studies demonstrate a significantly higher risk of neuropathy among patients with MGUS compared with matched controls. Establishing causality requires careful clinical phenotyping, correlation with electrodiagnostic studies, and systematic exclusion of alternative and potentially life-threatening causes that require urgent intervention. This review presents a practical, stepwise diagnostic and therapeutic framework for MGNS, emphasizing multidisciplinary collaboration between hematology and neurology. The approach is illustrated through two representative clinical scenarios: IgM MGUS with anti-myelin-associated glycoprotein (anti-MAG) neuropathy and non-IgM MGUS with chronic inflammatory demyelinating polyneuropathy (CIDP) or its variants. Treatment approaches differ fundamentally between these subtypes. B-cell-directed therapy with rituximab is the most commonly used first-line treatment for IgM anti-MAG neuropathy, whereas non-IgM MGNS with CIDP is managed with immunomodulatory strategies including intravenous immunoglobulin, corticosteroids, and plasmapheresis. We also discuss novel therapeutics, including Bruton tyrosine kinase inhibitors and complement-directed therapies. This framework provides clinicians with a structured approach to assess causal likelihood, stratify patients by neuropathy subtype, and guide evidence-based treatment decisions across the MGNS spectrum.

### [A] Intractable Nausea and Vomiting: A Key Feature of Neuromyelitis Optica Spectrum Disorder.

- PMID: 43002616
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory

- 来源类型: 今日新文献

- 期刊: The American journal of case reports
- 作者: Silva JM, Parmar J, Gandhi S, Gudiseva S
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 09
- DOI: 10.12659/AJCR.954044
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43002616/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-10

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND Area postrema syndrome (APS) can present with hiccups, nausea, or vomiting as a neurological presentation of neuromyelitis optica spectrum disorder (NMOSD). However, the diagnosis may be delayed and can result in neurological sequelae that include optic neuritis or myelitis. This report describes the case of a 22-year-old woman with intractable nausea, vomiting, and hiccups due to APS as a clinical manifestation of NMOSD. CASE REPORT A 22-year-old woman presented with a 5-week history of nausea, vomiting, and hiccups. After 3 emergency department visits, a first admission attributed her symptoms to gastroparesis on the basis of a gastric emptying study showing delayed emptying. Symptoms persisted despite antiemetic and prokinetic therapy, and she was readmitted 2 weeks later with new persistent hiccups and progressive bilateral upper-limb weakness. Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) of the head and cervical spine demonstrated an expansile T2-hyperintense lesion of the dorsal lower medulla and upper cervical cord. Given a high suspicion for a demyelinating attack, plasma exchange and high-dose intravenous corticosteroids were started before serology results returned. Serum aquaporin-4 immunoglobulin G (AQP4-IgG) was subsequently positive, establishing AQP4-IgG-positive NMOSD. Motor function improved after 7 plasma exchange sessions and rehabilitation, and she regained ambulation with a walker. CONCLUSIONS This report describes APS as the initial manifestation of NMOSD in a young woman whose intractable nausea, vomiting, and hiccups were first attributed to gastroparesis. NMOSD should be considered when unexplained nausea, vomiting, or hiccups persist beyond 48 hours, since prompt MRI and AQP4-IgG testing allow immunotherapy to begin without delay.

### [A] Disease burden among patients with generalized myasthenia gravis in the United States receiving standard of care therapy: the patient perspective.

- PMID: 43001852
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Independent plasma exchange/blood exchange research interest; not disease-domain relevance

- 主题: 换血/血浆置换疗法
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: Current medical research and opinion
- 作者: Pesa J, Jackson L, Keenan A, Gibson G, Connolly H, Birija SL, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 09
- DOI: 10.1080/03007995.2026.2757939
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43001852/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-10

#### 摘要

OBJECTIVE: Examination of disease burden experienced by patients with gMG treated with standard of care and not with advanced biologics can inform treatment decision-making.
METHODS: A cross-sectional, online survey was completed by adults with generalized myasthenia gravis (gMG; patients self-reported a confirmed diagnosis) in the United States. Patients had received treatment with standard of care (acetylcholinesterase inhibitors, non-steroidal immunosuppressants, corticosteroids, rituximab, immunoglobulins, plasmapheresis) but not advanced biologics (eculizumab, ravulizumab, zilucoplan, efgartigimod, rozanolixizumab, nipocalimab). Responses were compared between patients with mild or moderate/severe gMG (Myasthenia Gravis Activites of Daily Living [MG-ADL] score 0-4 or 5-24). Linear elastic-net regression analysis explored associations between MG-ADL score and selected variables.
RESULTS: Overall, 157 eligible patients completed the survey (87.3% female, mean [SD] age 49.5 [14.4] years, mean [SD] time since diagnosis 11.0 [10.9] years). Mean (SD) MG-QoL-15R score was 16.2 (7.2), EQ5D-5L score was 0.6 (0.3), and FACIT-fatigue subscale score was 20.2 (11.3). Overall, 51.6% received additional support/care. Patients with moderate/severe gMG (136/157 [86.6%]) had greater symptom occurence, reduced treatment satisfaction, greater health-related quality of life impairment, and greater support/care needs, compared with patients with mild gMG. In a regression analysis, gMG severity was associated with severity of health-related quality of life impairment (as measured by MG-ADL score and MG-QOL-15R score; coefficient 0.103 [95% CI 0.043, 0.191]).
CONCLUSION: Patients in the United States with gMG who are receiving only standard of care report a significant disease burden and impaired quality of life, with greater impairment among patients with severe gMG.

### [A] Oxygen-tension modulation and hyperbaric oxygen therapy as an investigational adjunct in Guillain-Barré syndrome: A narrative review.

- PMID: 43003781
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Independent plasma exchange/blood exchange research interest; not disease-domain relevance
- 优先级依据: A: plasma exchange/plasmapheresis/blood exchange/immunoadsorption
- 主题: 换血/血浆置换疗法
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: The Journal of international medical research
- 作者: Yuan B, Yang T, Bu X
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1177/03000605261502474
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43003781/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-10

#### 摘要

Guillain-Barré syndrome is an acute immune-mediated polyradiculoneuropathy. Severe disease and axonal variants may cause oxygen mismatch through edema, microvascular dysfunction, respiratory compromise, critical illness, and inflammatory metabolic demand. However, human studies of Guillain-Barré syndrome have not yet measured nerve-root oxygenation or microvascular changes before and after hyperbaric oxygen therapy. This narrative review evaluates whether oxygen-tension biology provides a framework for studying hyperbaric oxygen therapy as an adjunctive investigational intervention in selected Guillain-Barré syndrome phenotypes. Targeted searches of PubMed and CrossRef, updated through 20 August 2026 and conducted without a database language filter, were combined with reference tracking and appraised using narrative review transparency principles. The five reports included in this review comprise one case report involving a mechanically ventilated patient with Guillain-Barré syndrome, two acute motor axonal neuropathy case reports, one small retrospective series, and one acute motor axonal neuropathy animal model. Four of the five reports originate from the same author group and clinical setting and have not been independently reproduced. Mechanistic support comes mainly from experimental autoimmune neuritis, hyperbaric oxygen therapy-hypoxia-inducible factor studies outside Guillain-Barré syndrome, peripheral nerve injury research, and general hyperbaric oxygen therapy biology. Overall, oxygen-tension modulation is biologically plausible but remains unproven in severe or axonal Guillain-Barré syndrome. Hyperbaric oxygen therapy should not replace intravenous immunoglobulin, plasma exchange, respiratory support, or rehabilitation. It should be tested only as an adjunctive intervention, with phenotype selection, standardized protocols, independent replication, blinded outcome assessment, safety monitoring, and a candidate biomarker panel whose analytical feasibility is established before it is considered a core panel.

- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1016/j.chest.2026.05.008
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43003631/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-10

#### 摘要

A 64-year-old man who underwent bilateral orthotopic lung transplant for emphysema related to α1-antitrypsin deficiency 8 months ago presented to the clinic for assessment of bilateral pulmonary nodules identified on routine postlung transplant surveillance without any symptomatic concerns. The donor was positive for cytomegalovirus and positive for Epstein-Barr virus, and the recipient was negative for cytomegalovirus and positive for Epstein-Barr virus. He denied any fever, chills, cough, chest pain, dyspnea, weight loss, or night sweats. The patient lived in South Florida with no recent travel history outside the United States or infectious contacts. His posttransplant course was complicated with antibody-mediated rejection at 1.5 months after lung transplant. Antibody-mediated rejection was successfully treated with our institutional multimodal regimen, including a combination of plasmapheresis, carfilzomib, and anti-thymocyte globulin followed by monthly IV immunoglobulin and tocilizumab injections. Also, because of calcineurin inhibitor (CNI)-related renal dysfunction, the patient's immunosuppression was converted to a belatacept-based CNI-sparing regimen in addition to prednisone, with a tacrolimus goal trough of 4 to 6 ng/mL. He was also placed on mycophenolic acid, which was soon held because of neutropenia.

### [A] Acute motor-sensory axonal neuropathy with brain death-like manifestations: two case reports and literature review.

- PMID: 43000275
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Independent plasma exchange/blood exchange research interest; not disease-domain relevance
- 优先级依据: A: plasma exchange/plasmapheresis/blood exchange/immunoadsorption
- 主题: 换血/血浆置换疗法
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: BMC neurology
- 作者: Zhong L, Lei P, Chi Y, Liu X
- 发表日期: 2026 Aug 12
- DOI: 10.1186/s12883-026-05270-0
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43000275/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-10

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Acute motor-sensory axonal neuropathy (AMSAN) presenting with brain death-like features is rare, rapidly progressive, and easily mistaken early in the course for brain death of unclear cause or for locked-in syndrome. Misdiagnosis may delay timely immunotherapy and supportive care. We describe two such patients from a single center and summarize the published adult literature to improve recognition of this fulminant presentation.
CASE PRESENTATION: We retrospectively reviewed two patients treated at Yichang Central People's Hospital between 2016 and 2024 who developed brain death-like manifestations in the setting of AMSAN. We also searched CNKI, Wanfang Data, and PubMed for relevant reports published up to June 2025. Both patients developed rapidly progressive generalized paralysis, external and internal ophthalmoplegia, respiratory failure, and disappearance of brainstem reflexes, thereby simulating brain death. Electrophysiological studies showed severe motor-sensory axonal neuropathy and cerebrospinal fluid findings supported the diagnosis. One patient improved after intravenous immunoglobulin (IVIG), whereas the second showed no early response to IVIG but improved after subsequent plasma exchange. Our literature review identified 28 adult patients with fulminant Guillain-Barré syndrome presenting with brain death-like manifestations, most of whom required prolonged mechanical ventilation and experienced slow, incomplete motor recovery.
CONCLUSIONS: AMSAN presenting with brain death-like features is characterized by acute onset and complete systemic paralysis, typically initially manifesting as limb weakness and paralysis of both extraocular and intraocular muscles, followed by respiratory muscle paralysis. All patients require prolonged mechanical ventilation and intensive care treatment, rapidly presenting with clinical manifestations resembling "brain death." At this stage, electrophysiological studies and lumbar puncture are particularly crucial for definitive diagnosis. Early initiation of immunotherapy, such as IVIG infusion or plasma exchange, is critical. Patients often show gradual recovery of extraocular and intraocular muscles first; however, most experience poor recovery of respiratory muscle paralysis and limb weakness, significantly prolonging hospitalization and severely impacting patients' work and daily life, highlighting the need for prospective studies to evaluate therapeutic strategies beyond standard IVIG and plasma exchange. While emerging agents such as efgartigimod show preliminary promise in refractory GBS, current evidence is limited to small case reports, and prospective studies are critically needed.

### [A] Management of Immune-Mediated Thrombotic Thrombocytopenic Purpura Without Plasma Exchange in a Jehovah's Witness: A Case Report.

- PMID: 43005877
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory

- 作者: Wong C, Mehta R
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.1155/crh/2753797
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43005877/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-11

#### 摘要

Immune-mediated thrombotic thrombocytopenic purpura (iTTP) is a life-threatening thrombotic microangiopathy caused by severe ADAMTS13 deficiency and is traditionally treated with urgent therapeutic plasma exchange. Management becomes particularly challenging in patients who decline blood products. We report the case of a previously healthy woman with severe iTTP, with a platelet nadir of 6 × 109/L, ADAMTS13 activity < 5%, and an ADAMTS13 inhibitor titer of 2 Bethesda units, who declined plasma exchange because of her religious beliefs as a member of Jehovah's Witnesses. She was treated with high-dose corticosteroids, rituximab, and adjunctive intravenous immunoglobulin (IVIG). Platelet count normalized by hospital Day 9 with progressive resolution of hemolysis, and at 6-month follow-up the patient remained in durable clinical and biochemical remission with normalization of ADAMTS13 activity. This case highlights the potential adjunctive role of IVIG within an immune-directed treatment strategy for iTTP when plasma exchange is not feasible.
